Source files and the schema were re-read for this phase. The development answer keys were not opened. Session 01 established the input statistics below using input files only.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Verified requirement or observation | Consequence for the design |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| The output contains exactly `people`, `organisations`, `chats`, `goals`, and `open_loops`. | Internal debugging and confidence fields must be removed at export. |\\r\\n| People have one allowed circle; `ME` is excluded from the people array. | Maintain one global classification per input person rather than different circles in different chats. |\\r\\n| Every chat maps to exactly one person or organisation. | Final coverage and uniqueness checks are mandatory. A group is not automatically an organisation. |\\r\\n| Organisation IDs are solution-defined and matched through attached chats and people. | Focus on correct grouping rather than reproducing development names. |\\r\\n| Goals have evidence and at most ten entries. | Emit supported goals, not a quota of ten guesses. |\\r\\n| Loops have source, owner, type, and final status; done/dropped work belongs in the output too. | Keep completed and cancelled tasks in the ledger rather than deleting them. |\\r\\n| The brief asks for closure evidence and action-specific assistance details. | Enforce those semantics in addition to the schema's weaker optional-field rules. |\\r\\n| The checker is hand-written and does not load the JSON Schema. | Passing the checker is not sufficient validation. |\\r\\n| Dev has 4,502 messages, 138 chats, 286 other participants, ten seeded circles, two organisation names, and no seeded goals. | Read actual seed content; do not assume the quantities in illustrative descriptions. |\\r\\n| Dev has no non-null quoted IDs and contains media placeholders. | Missing reply structure and attachment content are normal conditions, not fatal errors. |\\r\\n| Hidden evaluation is approximately 15,000 messages over six later weeks, with names changed again. | Rules and prompts must generalise from evidence rather than memorised names or IDs. |\\r\\n| Loop evaluation concerns the hidden set's last two weeks; earlier weeks provide context. | Process the whole supplied history. Do not read answer metadata or discard early evidence. |\\r\\n| Goals and help decisions are unlabelled in dev; other labels are incomplete/imperfect according to the data README. | Report official and manually reviewed quality separately. |\\r\\n| The run has a 30-minute limit in a clean Linux environment, with network access only to the model endpoint. | No runtime package downloads, web search, model downloads, or external retrieval services. |\\r\\n| Model configuration is supplied through `LLM_BASE_URL`, `LLM_API_KEY`, and `LLM_MODEL`. | Do not hardcode a second endpoint or silently switch models. |\\r\\n| Fine-tuned and self-hosted models are prohibited by the supplied PDF. | Choose a hosted compatible model; ordinary local processing remains available. |\\r\\n\\r\\nThe full point allocation is circles 12, organisations 8, chats 8, goals 8, loops found 14, loop status 14, help 10, cost 12, write-up 7, and one-command execution 7. The loop-related components total 38 points, relationships/organisations/chats total 28, and cost plus execution total 19. These are prioritisation inputs, not permission to omit required output fields.\\r\\n\\r\\nAll chunk sizes, budget allocations, retry limits, confidence rules, module names, and acceptance targets below are proposed engineering choices. None is an invented challenge requirement. No measured accuracy, cost, or runtime is claimed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Overall components and end-to-end data flow.**\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Configuration and input gate: resolve the three allowed input filenames, runtime settings, and the configured endpoint. Record a fingerprint of the run inputs and configuration. Never recursively discover or load answer files.\\r\\n2. Normalised input store: retain original records and construct indexes by message ID, chat ID, person ID, and timestamp. This is the immutable evidence store: derived decisions must not alter the source records.\\r\\n3. Context planner: divide chat histories into bounded conversation windows, attach relevant seed facts, build reply/participant references, and record which messages each window covers.\\r\\n4. Shared evidence extractor: use an LLM to identify relationship clues, organisation clues, goal candidates, new tasks, and task-update events within each window. Its output is intermediate structured JSON, not the final submission object.\\r\\n5. Evidence validator and merger: check all cited IDs and spans, combine observations from overlapping windows, and retain ambiguous updates for later resolution.\\r\\n6. Relationship resolver: combine evidence across chats to decide circles, organisation membership, staff care links, and chat assignments. Resolve organisations and people jointly in a bounded sequence rather than assuming either is already perfect.\\r\\n7. Goal resolver: merge candidates, verify the user's intention and evidence, assign allowed categories/horizons, and rank no more than ten supported goals.\\r\\n8. Task reconciler: link observations to existing tasks, track updates in chronological order, and use focused LLM review for ambiguous matching, completion, cancellation, or reopening.\\r\\n9. Help policy: consider the final open tasks, ownership, sensitivity, and useful next action; choose assist, remind, or leave with the requested details.\\r\\n10. Final validation and export: enforce the actual schema, identifier integrity, complete coverage, and semantic invariants; sort deterministically and write a single output file safely.\\r\\n11. Independent evaluation: only after an approved inference run, compare its frozen output with references in a separate evaluation process. This stage is never imported by the inference pipeline.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe model gateway, request cache, usage ledger, and runtime scheduler support steps 4â€“9. They do not decide what constitutes a family relationship or task completion. The pipeline owns those responsibilities explicitly.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe main dependencies are: raw inputs â†’ windows â†’ evidence; evidence â†’ relationship directory and task/goal candidates; relationship directory plus task events â†’ reconciled tasks; reconciled tasks plus relationship directory â†’ help; all resolved categories â†’ validation â†’ final JSON. Goals can be resolved alongside task reconciliation once relationship evidence is available. There is no need for a single global LLM call containing the entire history.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Loading and preprocessing.**\\r\\n\\r\\nUse UTF-8 and read JSONL one nonblank line at a time. A JSONL file contains an independent JSON record per line. Retain original text, IDs, sender identity, timestamp, kind, and quoted ID. Store original line position for reproducible ordering when timestamps tie. Reject duplicate conflicting IDs and missing essential fields with clear diagnostics rather than silently overwriting records.\\r\\n\\r\\nBuild a person registry from chat participants, excluding `ME`, and index senders as evidence about those IDs. Report a sender absent from all participant metadata instead of inventing a new identity mapping. Keep zero-message chats and silent participants: the final coverage rules still apply to them. Parse seed circles as authoritative supplied facts; seed organisation names are anchors, not fully specified organisation records. A seed goal still needs actual message evidence before it can satisfy the output goal schema.\\r\\n\\r\\nGroup messages by `chat_id`, then stable-sort by timestamp and original line position. Use the input's maximum valid timestamp as the global end-of-data time, called `as_of`. This gives reproducible final status and reminder planning. Do not use today's wall-clock date to interpret a historical conversation. Use a monotonic clock separately to enforce runtime budgets; that clock must not affect inferred due dates.\\r\\n\\r\\nPreserve raw content and construct a separate normalised search representation. Unicode normalisation, case folding for matching, and whitespace handling can improve retrieval, but must not overwrite the original evidence. Do not indiscriminately strip emojis, punctuation, numbers, negation, or short replies: they can change meaning. Repeated identical text with different IDs remains distinct evidence. Forwarded advertisements can be marked as likely low relevance, but should not be silently dropped before any semantic reading.\\r\\n\\r\\nRepresent explicit dates and relative date phrases separately internally. Python can convert a resolved calendar expression into a date. An LLM is useful for ambiguity such as whether Hindi `kal` means yesterday or tomorrow in context. Do not mechanically resolve every relative expression using a keyword dictionary. When a due date cannot be justified, retain null rather than invent one.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe dataset's semantic timezone is not explicitly specified in the inspected materials. Proposed initial policy: a documented configurable Asia/Kolkata default, recognising explicit timezone statements when present. This is an assumption awaiting confirmation, not a rule of the challenge. Preserve UTC timestamps regardless of the local-time choice. Overdue tasks remain open unless there is closure evidence; a passed deadline is not proof of completion.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Conversation windows and evidence preservation.**\\r\\n\\r\\nStart with windows bounded by estimated tokens, not a fixed number of messages. A one-word reply and a lengthy document should not count as equal context cost. A tentative starting range is 3,000â€“6,000 input tokens per extraction window, leaving room for instructions, retrieved context, and output. Tune this using measured extraction density and latency. A model's advertised maximum context is a ceiling, not a recommended prompt size.\\r\\n\\r\\nPrefer natural boundaries, such as a substantial time gap or topic shift, while retaining a small overlap of neighbouring turns. A tentative overlap is up to 10â€“15% of window tokens, adjusted when a question/reply pair would otherwise be split. Keep explicit quoted antecedents where present; if an antecedent lies outside the window, retrieve it by ID. Label overlap messages as context and primary messages as the window's extraction responsibility.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe window manifest tracks every input message's primary window and whether that window was processed successfully. Primary coverage is distinct from repeated context coverage. No keyword-only filter should decide that a message can never contain a goal or loop. Initially, every message is eligible for the shared extraction pass; later cost reductions require an audited estimate of missed items. Very long messages are divided into overlapping spans under the same original message ID, with offsets for traceability rather than a silently truncated body.\\r\\n\\r\\nEach observation records source IDs, any short supporting span, its subject, and its proposed interpretation. Retain the source window and prompt version. Verify that IDs exist and that quoted spans correspond to those messages. A valid citation is necessary but not sufficient: later review must also check that the cited words support the claim. Never ask a model to manufacture message IDs from memory.\\r\\n\\r\\nWindows may be extracted independently for parallelism. If an isolated update such as a short completion reply cannot be assigned locally, preserve it as an unresolved update event rather than dropping it or inventing an antecedent. The later task reconciler uses chronology, nearby messages, known participants, and a ledger of candidate tasks to resolve it. This avoids a long serial chain of LLM summaries while retaining cross-window continuity.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen replies are missing, adjacency and topic similarity are clues, not proof of a reply relation. Keep internal candidate links and their supporting evidence; never rewrite an absent `quoted_id` as an established fact. A message containing only a media placeholder supplies its existence, sender, kind, and time, not the attachment's contents. Nearby text may establish what it means. The system should not claim to have read a receipt, document, or photograph that is unavailable.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Multilingual handling and bounded retrieval.**\\r\\n\\r\\nPass English, Hindi, Bengali, and Hinglish text in its original script to a model benchmarked on those languages. Avoid translating the whole dataset up front: translation adds cost and can change kinship terms, negation, tense, or politeness. For a genuinely ambiguous phrase, a selective model review may include an internal translation alongside the original; evidence always points to the original message. Language detection is a routing clue, not a reason to reject mixed-script messages. Do not infer a person's relationship from language, surname, or an honorific alone.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep compact per-chat and per-person evidence cards. A card is a short list of supported facts and unresolved alternatives with source IDs, not an unrestricted prose biography. Retrieve relevant original messages when deciding a conflict. Maintain three context layers: local conversation turns, compact relationship/task state, and targeted original evidence. Summaries must never become the sole proof of a final claim.\\r\\n\\r\\nStart retrieval with IDs, participants, organisation aliases, time range, and character/token overlap. A character-based index can handle script and spelling variation without an additional embedding endpoint. Keyword matching is only for finding candidate evidence, not classifying semantics. Add embeddings later only if measured recall failures justify the dependency, model availability, cost, and allowed endpoint. An embeddings API cannot be assumed from Chat Completions compatibility or from a single configured chat model.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf a compact context packet exceeds its budget, partition the relevant candidates and evaluate all partitions as needed; do not silently evict old unresolved tasks. Completed tasks remain searchable for later revisions or reopening. Retrieval limits must be measured against missed links, especially for short replies. Evidence cards and indexes make memory manageable, but no finite prompt budget guarantees perfect recall.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Internal data model and uncertainty.**\\r\\n\\r\\nUse simple typed records and dictionaries initially; a graph database and vector service are unnecessary. A graph here simply means relationships between objects, represented by IDs and lists in ordinary Python.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Internal record | Contents and responsibility |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Input record | Original message/chat fields and immutable provenance. |\\r\\n| Evidence observation | Subject, proposed fact, message IDs/spans, extraction window, alternatives, and support strength. |\\r\\n| Person profile | Seed circle if supplied; candidate circles; organisation links; care links; contradictions; supporting IDs. |\\r\\n| Organisation candidate | Name aliases, associated people/chats, type/side/role hypotheses, merge evidence. |\\r\\n| Chat profile | Participants, purpose evidence, candidate person/organisation targets, final choice. |\\r\\n| Goal candidate | Intention, area/type/horizon candidates, ownership by the user, evidence, deduplication group. |\\r\\n| Task record | Stable internal ID, canonical source, additional supporting origins, owner, action/object, due-date evidence, event history, current status, closure evidence. |\\r\\n| Task event | Creation, acceptance, update, completion, cancellation, or reopening interpretation, with timestamp and supporting IDs. |\\r\\n| Decision record | Final interpretation, evidence, uncertainty reason, and whether a fallback was used. |\\r\\n| Request/run record | Input/config/prompt fingerprints, tokens, elapsed time, errors, retries, model identity, coverage, and cache status. |\\r\\n\\r\\nAn LLM-generated confidence number is not a calibrated probability. Prefer interpretable support categories: explicit statement, repeated contextual support, weak indirect clue, or unresolved contradiction. If numeric thresholds are later us…17089 tokens truncated…window coverage, batching, event replay, output size, memory, and export. Include one large chat and long messages, not just many tiny chats. Record actual local elapsed time and memory observations. This checks local overhead and shutdown behavior, not live model latency or quality. |\\r\\n| 6. A bounded live pilot is separately approved and reviewed | With a specific small spending/call cap, use the intended endpoint/model and the same code, prompt versions, and response schemas planned for the full run. Obtain at least one valid response for each request family, exercise every output category across the chosen cases, and record observed usage, latency, repair behavior, and available response metadata. Reuse qualifying recent pilot evidence only if the relevant build/configuration is unchanged. |\\r\\n| 7. The pilot's critical semantic checks pass | Predeclare a small review set covering English, Hindi, Bengali, Hinglish, and the critical patterns: acknowledgement without closure, cancellation under negation, completion/reopening, ownership, explicit cross-chat matching, and unsupported goals/help. Cases may be synthetic where the available input lacks a language/pattern. Manually inspect the raw supporting messages and outputs. Resolve observed fabricated evidence, false closure on the declared cases, missing required category handling, and ignored input before scaling. Document other errors and uncertainty; do not invent an overall accuracy threshold or claim hidden-set performance from this small set. |\\r\\n| 8. A concrete full-run budget and time forecast fit | Perform input-only planning for the intended dataset: count actual windows and use pilot observation density/usage/latency to estimate downstream job ranges. Include retries, output allowances, cross-chat review, all five categories, and finalization reserves. Show a conservative upper planning estimate using the slower/larger observed cases and an explicit safety allowance, not just an average. The planned model work must fit before the 27-minute network cutoff, with the section 9a exit margin intact. If evidence is too sparse or the forecast exceeds time/token/spend limits, do not scale yet. |\\r\\n| 9. The user approves the specific run | Present the exact dataset path/count, model and endpoint identity without secrets, code/configuration/prompt versions, estimated call/token ranges, verified pricing or other enforceable spend controls, maximum authorized spend, hard call/token/time limits, expected output path, and known pilot weaknesses. Obtain explicit full-run approval or point to an existing approval that clearly covers this exact scope and budget. |\\r\\n\\r\\nThe live pilot itself must not start without its own approved cap. Prefer synthetic cases first; any use of development messages must be limited to allowed inputs and the configured endpoint. No cached answer labels, manual-review labels, or benchmark findings are supplied as inference facts. The pilot is intended to verify compatibility and identify obvious failures cheaply, not to become a disguised full-dataset run.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf returned token usage is absent, do not report a measured token cost. A paid full run can proceed only if its approved maximum spend is still enforceable using verified provider billing/limits or conservative request/output reservations backed by known pricing. Otherwise this gate fails until the uncertainty is resolved. Missing prices must not be replaced with a hypothetical dollar figure. Unknown account quotas and too few latency samples weaken the runtime forecast; disclose them and use a smaller additional approved pilot when necessary.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep one concise readiness record with pass/fail evidence for gates 1â€“8 and the user's gate-9 authorization. This can be part of the existing run documentation; it needs no extra application module or dashboard. Do not require a successful full paid run before requesting its first authorization: that would be circular. The authorized full run is H2 and supplies the actual end-to-end cost/runtime/coverage evidence for later submission readiness. It may still fail under new workload or service behavior, in which case preserve diagnostics, stop within limits, and do not automatically purchase a rerun.\\r\\n\\r\\nMinimum acceptance-gate tests use recorded/mock readiness results: a missing goal-ranking test, failed Linux cleanup, unresolved mandatory stage, absent pilot authority, over-budget projection, or missing full-run approval must each prevent a full-run-ready decision. A fully satisfied synthetic checklist should produce readiness-to-request-approval only until explicit budget authority exists. These tests verify the gate's mechanics; they do not fabricate passed real-world tests.\\r\\n\\r\\n**12. Essential version 1 versus optional improvements.**\\r\\n\\r\\n| Essential first version | Defer until a measured need appears |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Six modules and the existing two-argument entry point. | A package hierarchy with a separate module for every small responsibility. |\\r\\n| All five output categories and help decisions. | Multiple models, providers, or independent agents. |\\r\\n| Raw evidence IDs, full primary coverage, compact context, chronological task ledger. | Vector databases, embedding models, and database persistence. |\\r\\n| Same-chat matching and evidence-triggered cross-chat matching. | Broad semantic cross-chat search with embeddings. |\\r\\n| Bounded retries/repair/splitting, one network boundary, hard request deadlines. | Resume/checkpoint infrastructure and provider-specific batch services. |\\r\\n| Run-local identical-request reuse and usage/time counters. | Persistent result caches, provider-prefix cache optimisation, and dashboards. |\\r\\n| One selected offline validator backend plus project semantic checks, following section 1. | Parallel production validator implementations or automatic runtime backend switching. |\\r\\n| Original-language processing and selected raw-evidence retrieval. | Whole-history translation and additional language-detection services. |\\r\\n| Synthetic regression tests, Linux deadline tests, a bounded approved pilot, and an explicit paid-full-run gate; measured cold-run readiness after approval. | Large automated model leaderboards or repeated full-run tuning without a specific hypothesis. |\\r\\n\\r\\nThe practical implementation order is A/B input isolation â†’ C validation/export â†’ D client/budgets â†’ E extraction/context â†’ F task ledger â†’ G help/integration â†’ H1 readiness and full-run approval â†’ H2 authorized full-dataset rehearsal. That order makes errors observable before complex reasoning is added. A small skeleton run with mocked replies precedes paid testing; a separately approved bounded pilot precedes the full-run authorization decision.\\r\\n\\r\\n**13. Decisions still needed and explicit limitations.**\\r\\n\\r\\n- Model/endpoint identity, supported parameters, context/output limits, account quotas, and pricing must be verified before a full paid run. This revision recommends no model as proven best and repeats no unverified capability claims.\\r\\n- The user's approved testing budget and a corresponding token ceiling are still needed before paid testing. This does not block designing or later testing pure Python with mocks after implementation approval.\\r\\n- Relative-date timezone and reminder scheduling remain documented policies to agree. No current date is substituted for the dataset endpoint.\\r\\n- The exact cost-to-points rubric remains unknown. Optimise measured tokens/time and semantic quality, not an invented score formula.\\r\\n- Lexical/evidence-triggered cross-chat matching can miss implicit paraphrases. Track unresolved events and review a sample before adding more retrieval infrastructure.\\r\\n- No method can recover attachment contents absent from the inputs. Weak evidence and forced schema categories remain real uncertainty.\\r\\n- A provider outage or an input exceeding measured capacity can prevent complete inference within 30 minutes. The design bounds execution and reports failure; it cannot guarantee accurate full output under arbitrary service failure.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is the revised implementation plan for review. It simplifies the earlier architecture without removing any required output category or evidence protections. The earlier architecture report remains an unchanged record; this separate document is the proposed first-version implementation scope. Implementation, solution execution, API calls, and answer-based evaluation await approval.\\r\\n# Progress checkpoint â€” Stage A completed\\r\\n\\r\\n## Current status\\r\\n\\r\\n**Stage A is complete at the data-module level**, including primary assignment and the latest context-validation correction. Context must now be an ordered immediately preceding suffix within its chat and configured overlap limit. The existing loader, splitter, and exactly-once primary checks were not rewritten. Stage B has not started.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe earlier checkpoint correctly identified a gap; this continuation closes it. Assignment coverage is not extraction completion. The implementation report now records the new work above its preserved historical report; the architecture plan is unchanged.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Completed work and files\\r\\n\\r\\n- `data.py`: existing loader/indexes/evidence retained; added `MessageSpan`, `ProcessingWindow`, `WindowPlan`, `build_windows()`, and `validate_window_coverage()`. Exactly-once primary coverage, separate preceding context, original IDs, and explicit partial-input status are now available.\\r\\n- `tests/test_data.py`: 44 synthetic tests: the original 25, 14 window/coverage tests, and 5 latest context-validation tests. These include corrupted assignments, a 30-configuration packing/reconstruction matrix, stale/reversed/excessive context, custom limits, and per-chat overlap history.\\r\\n- `ai-logs/codex/session-03-implementation.md`: updated with this continuation's decisions, functions, actual test result, and limitations.\\r\\n- `ai-logs/codex/session-03-transcript.md`: append-only continuation of accessible actual dialogue, including the previous clarification's final response; existing bytes preserved.\\r\\n- `ai-logs/codex/progress-checkpoint.md`: this updated readiness record.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification\\r\\n\\r\\nThe new test run was on local Windows/Python 3.12. Earlier results below remain historical and were not rerun on development data:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Check | Actual recorded result |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| **Current:** `python -B -m unittest discover -s tests -v` | **44 tests, 0.475 seconds, `OK`, exit 0.** All previous 39 tests and 5 new tests passed. |\\r\\n| Before this correction: `python -B -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_data.py -v -k context_overlap_rejects` | 3 regression tests, 0.034 seconds, `FAILED (failures=4)`, exit 1. Reproduced acceptance of invalid context, including two stale/skipped-predecessor subcases. |\\r\\n| Earlier window implementation: `python -B -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_data.py -v` | 39 tests, 0.539 seconds, `OK`, exit 0. |\\r\\n| `python -B -m unittest discover -s tests -v -f` | 25 tests, 0.352 seconds, `OK`, exit 0, Windows/Python 3.12. |\\r\\n| `python -B data.py '..\\\\..\\\\xpert-cold-start\\\\data\\\\dev'` | `succeeded`, 4,502 messages seen/parsed/indexed with valid timestamps; 138 chats; 286 people; no issues or rejected records; exit 0. |\\r\\n| Original implementation-session preservation check | All 17 snapshotted pre-existing starter-project files unchanged. |\\r\\n\\r\\nThe two preliminary permission failures belong to the original loader session. This correction first reproduced the context bug, then passed the complete synthetic suite after the fix. Durations are actual unittest observations, not benchmarks. No solution execution, model calls, checker runs, answer-file access, or development-dataset window run occurred in this correction.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Primary coverage: plan requirement versus implemented behaviour\\r\\n\\r\\nThe approved plan explicitly places primary assignment in Stage A. Section 11's Stage A row includes long-message spans and states: â€œEvery primary message/span is covered onceâ€. Section 3 requires exactly one primary assignment per message or long-message span, with overlap excluded from extra coverage. Section 1 assigns window creation to `data.py`; section 2 separates input loading (step 1), window/job creation (step 2), and extraction (step 3). These are plan facts, not a newly proposed redesign.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Coverage layer | Meaning | Owner and current status |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Loading/indexing | Which nonblank input records were read, parsed, retained, rejected, and uniquely indexed? | Stage A, `data.py`: implemented. A successful load does not prove extraction happened. |\\r\\n| Primary window/span assignment | Does each message, or every span of a long message, belong to exactly one primary extraction window? Context overlap must not count twice; spans must preserve original message IDs. | Stage A, `data.py`: implemented and tested; validation checks original-body intervals independently of splitting. |\\r\\n| Processing/extraction | Did each required window/job complete with validated coverage acknowledgement, rather than fail or remain pending? | Stage E extraction, with Stage G orchestration/completion checks in `solution.py`: future work. Acknowledgement alone does not prove that the model found every relevant fact. |\\r\\n\\r\\nUnsplit messages have one primary window; a long message can span multiple windows, with each nonoverlapping span assigned exactly once under the same original ID. Empty bodies get one [0, 0) span. Undated messages are retained; failed/incomplete input cannot pass `WindowPlan.require_complete()`, even when every indexed message has an assignment. Rejected source lines remain explicit and are not given fabricated evidence IDs.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe validator now enforces context against a bounded ordered per-chat tail, rather than global earlier-primary membership. Nonempty context must be a contiguous immediate suffix in order, with length at most the configured `overlap_spans`; empty context is allowed. This tail may cross earlier window boundaries, matching the unchanged builder. The builder passes its limit to validation, and the coverage report records that limit for later explicit revalidation. Stale, skipped, reversed, excessive, and wrong-chat context fail without changing primary coverage counts.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Remaining work and next stage\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Stage A:** no outstanding implementation requirement identified within this scope. The loading, evidence, ordering, and primary assignment gates are covered by synthetic tests.\\r\\n- **Stage B â€” next named stage:** broader no-answer boundary tests with synthetic decoys, intercepted opens/no scans, and independence of outputs/request payloads from decoys. Not started; awaits user approval.\\r\\n- **Stage E / G:** extraction-job completion tracking and final integration of stage/coverage gates. The baseline runner currently does not call the new loader.\\r\\n- **Platform/readiness work:** Linux and Python 3.9 compatibility remain unverified. The plan places Linux execution/deadline readiness checks in section 9a and Stage H1; Stage A's Windows test result is not Linux certification.\\r\\n- **Local cleanup:** inaccessible directories from preliminary tests remain a known limitation. The previous report records 25 under `tests/stage-a-*`; first-attempt system-temp leftovers were not enumerated. No cleanup was attempted again in this turn.\\r\\n- Original parsed dictionaries remain mutable; later consumers must avoid altering evidence records. Unknown dates remain undated rather than guessed.\\r\\n- Window limits are configurable Python-character/span bounds, not token budgets: defaults are 4,000 primary characters, 32 primary spans, and up to 2 preceding context spans. Metadata/prompt tokens and extraction acknowledgement are later-stage responsibilities. Fixed character splits can cross words or Unicode grapheme boundaries; original text is retained exactly and can be reconstructed.\\r\\n- The coverage dictionary is a snapshot. Revalidate against the original input if a future consumer changes assignments; do not mutate preserved input or coverage reports. The standalone CLI still reports loading only.\\r\\n\\r\\nStop here pending approval. No Stage B or change to the six-module architecture was performed.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n## Session 04 update - Stage B ready for review\\r\\n\\r\\nThis appended entry supersedes the earlier \\\"Stage B not started\\\" status above;\\r\\nall preceding checkpoint history is preserved. Stage A remains approved and\\r\\nunchanged. Stage B now has six deterministic tests in\\r\\n`tests/test_no_answer_boundary.py`, including 19 negative-control subcases.\\r\\n\\r\\nSynthetic inputs and decoys are isolated in test-owned directories. Guards cover\\r\\nmodule initialization and execution, intercept file opens, reject directory scans\\r\\nand checker imports/execution, and retain violations even when exceptions are\\r\\ncaught. Only the three input files are readable; output and usage paths are\\r\\nwrite-only. No real answers or real model/API calls were used.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe baseline still lacks model orchestration. Stage B therefore compares actual\\r\\nbaseline output bytes separately from a test-only loader/window-to-client probe\\r\\nwith one nonempty wire request and a fixed reply. Identical inputs with/without\\r\\ndecoys produce identical requests, replies, baseline outputs, and usage logs.\\r\\nA changed permitted participant changes requests and baseline output. Future\\r\\nintegration must apply these boundary checks to its actual orchestration; this\\r\\nentry does not claim completion of later stages.\\r\\n\\r\\nVerified on Windows/Python 3.12.9, from `xpert-cold-start-starter/cold-start`:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Exact command | Actual result |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| `python -B -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_no_answer_boundary.py -v` | 6 tests, 7.998s, OK, exit 0 |\\r\\n| `python -B -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_data.py -v` | 44 tests, 0.677s, OK, exit 0 |\\r\\n| `python -B -m unittest discover -s tests -v` | 50 tests, 8.390s, OK, exit 0 |\\r\\n\\r\\nThree initial Stage B harness runs failed (9, 17, then 1 unittest failures).\\r\\nWindows access-mask handling, codec preloading, and per-run artifact reset were\\r\\ncorrected; all final runs pass. Exact failed-run durations and commands are in\\r\\n`session-04-implementation.md` and `session-04-transcript.md`. No Stage B fixture\\r\\ndirectories remained after cleanup.  \"extra_input\": lambda: (folder / \"extra.json\").read_bytes(),\r\n                \"output_read\": lambda: output.read_bytes(),\r\n                \"listdir\": lambda: os.listdir(folder),\r\n                \"scandir\": lambda: os.scandir(folder),\r\n                \"iterdir\": lambda: list(folder.iterdir()),\r\n                \"glob\": lambda: glob.glob(str(folder / \"*.json\")),\r\n                \"iglob\": lambda: list(glob.iglob(str(folder / \"**\"), recursive=True)),\r\n                \"path_glob\": lambda: list(folder.glob(\"*\")),\r\n                \"rglob\": lambda: list(folder.rglob(\"*\")),\r\n                \"walk\": lambda: list(os.walk(folder)),\r\n                \"import_check\": lambda: __import__(\"check\"),\r\n                \"importlib_check\": lambda: importlib.import_module(\"check\"),\r\n                \"cached_check\": lambda: importlib.import_module(\"check\"),\r\n                \"exec_check\": lambda: exec(compile(\"pass\", str(folder / \"check.py\"), \"exec\")),\r\n            }\r\n            if attack == \"cached_check\":\r\n                sys.modules[\"check\"] = types.ModuleType(\"check\")\r\n            if attack in attacks:\r\n                # Deliberately swallow the exception: the recorded violation\r\n                # must still make the probe fail (as in broad runtime handlers).\r\n                try:\r\n                    attacks[attack]()\r\n                except AssertionError:\r\n                    pass\r\n\r\n            phase = \"loader\"\r\n            data = modules[\"data\"].load_input(folder)\r\n            data.require_complete()\r\n            windows = modules[\"data\"].build_windows(data)\r\n            windows.require_complete()\r\n            # Test contract only; no production prompt/extractor is introduced.\r\n            payload = {\"chats\": [r.original for r in data.chat_records],\r\n                       \"messages\": [r.original for r in data.message_records],\r\n                       \"seed\": data.seed,\r\n                       \"windows\": [dataclasses.asdict(w) for w in windows.windows]}\r\n            phase = \"model\"\r\n            # Relative usage.jsonl must land in the owned synthetic directory.\r\n            os.chdir(folder)\r\n            with patch.dict(os.environ, {\"LLM_BASE_URL\": \"https://example.invalid/v1\",\r\n                                         \"LLM_MODEL\": \"synthetic-model\",\r\n                                         \"LLM_API_KEY\": \"synthetic-not-a-credential\"}), \\\r\n                    patch.object(urllib.request, \"urlopen\", fixed_transport), \\\r\n                    patch.object(time, \"time\", side_effect=[10.0, 10.25]):\r\n                report[\"model_output\"] = modules[\"llm\"].chat(\r\n                    [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": json.dumps(payload, ensure_ascii=False)}],\r\n                    temperature=0, max_tokens=64, response_format={\"type\": \"json_object\"})\r\n            phase = \"solution\"\r\n            modules[\"solution\"].main(str(folder), str(output))\r\n            report[\"ok\"] = not report[\"violations\"]\r\n    except Exception as error:\r\n        report[\"error\"] = type(error).__name__ + \": \" + str(error)\r\n    finally:\r\n        active = False\r\n    if report[\"ok\"]:\r\n        report[\"output\"] = output.read_bytes().hex()\r\n        report[\"usage\"] = usage.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\r\n    return report\r\n\r\n\r\nclass NoAnswerBoundaryTests(unittest.TestCase):\r\n    def setUp(self):\r\n        # Inherit workspace permissions, as Stage A does. Cleanup only this\r\n        # newly created, explicitly owned directory after the child exits.\r\n        self.folder = PROJECT / \"tests\" / (\"stage-b-\" + uuid.uuid4().hex)\r\n        self.folder.mkdir()\r\n        self.addCleanup(shutil.rmtree, self.folder)\r\n        chats = [{\"chat_id\": \"C1\", \"name\": \"Synthetic group\", \"is_group\": True,\r\n                  \"participants\": [\"ME\", \"P1\"]},\r\n                 {\"chat_id\": \"C2\", \"name\": \"Synthetic direct\", \"is_group\": False,\r\n                  \"participants\": [\"ME\", \"P2\"]}]\r\n        messages = [{\"id\": \"M1\", \"chat_id\": \"C1\", \"sender_id\": \"P1\",\r\n                     \"sender_name\": \"Synthetic person\", \"ts\": 100, \"kind\": \"text\",\r\n                     \"body\": \"Synthetic message à¤¨à¤®à¤¸à¥à¤¤à¥‡\", \"quoted_id\": None}]\r\n        for name, rows in ((\"chats.jsonl\", chats), (\"messages.jsonl\", messages)):\r\n            (self.folder / name).write_text(\r\n                \"\".join(json.dumps(row, ensure_ascii=False) + \"\\n\" for row in rows), encoding=\"utf-8\")\r\n        (self.folder / \"seed.json\").write_text('{\"circles\": {\"P1\": \"family\"}}', encoding=\"utf-8\")\r\n\r\n    def add_decoys(self):\r\n        answers = self.folder / \"answers\"\r\n        answers.mkdir()\r\n        for name in (\"people\", \"organisations\", \"chats\", \"goals\", \"open_loops\"):\r\n            (answers / (name + \".json\")).write_text(json.dumps({\"decoy\": DECOY}), encoding=\"utf-8\")\r\n        (self.folder / \"extra.json\").write_text(DECOY, encoding=\"utf-8\")\r\n        (self.folder / \"check.py\").write_text(\"raise AssertionError('synthetic checker executed')\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\r\n\r\n    def run_probe(self, attack=\"\"):\r\n        # Give each run the same initial artifact state. The client appends its\r\n        # usage log; comparing cumulative logs would test fixture reuse instead.\r\n        for name in (\"output.json\", \"usage.jsonl\"):\r\n            (self.folder / name).unlink(missing_ok=True)\r\n        result = subprocess.run(\r\n            [sys.executable, \"-B\", str(Path(__file__).resolve()), \"--probe\", str(self.folder), attack],\r\n            cwd=PROJECT, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding=\"utf-8\", timeout=30)\r\n        self.assertEqual(result.returncode, 0, result.stderr)\r\n        return json.loads(result.stdout)\r\n\r\n    def assert_clean(self, result):\r\n        self.assertTrue(result[\"ok\"], result)\r\n        self.assertEqual(result[\"violations\"], [])\r\n        self.assertEqual(Counter(name for phase, name in result[\"reads\"] if phase == \"loader\"),\r\n                         Counter(INPUTS))\r\n        self.assertEqual(set(name for _, name in result[\"reads\"]), set(INPUTS))\r\n        self.assertEqual(result[\"writes\"], [[\"model\", \"usage.jsonl\"], [\"solution\", \"output.json\"]])\r\n        self.assertFalse(any(\"check\" in name.split(\".\") for name in result[\"imports\"]))\r\n\r\n    def test_inference_reads_only_allowed_files_without_scans(self):\r\n        self.add_decoys()\r\n        result = self.run_probe()\r\n        self.assert_clean(result)\r\n        self.assertEqual([name for phase, name in result[\"reads\"] if phase == \"solution\"],\r\n                         [\"chats.jsonl\", \"messages.jsonl\"])\r\n\r\n    def test_requests_and_final_outputs_are_identical_with_and_without_decoys(self):\r\n        before = self.run_probe()\r\n        self.add_decoys()\r\n        after = self.run_probe()\r\n        for result in (before, after):\r\n            self.assert_clean(result)\r\n            self.assertEqual(len(result[\"requests\"]), 1)  # Not an empty-payload comparison.\r\n            body = json.loads(result[\"requests\"][0])\r\n            content = json.loads(body[\"messages\"][0][\"content\"])\r\n            self.assertEqual(content[\"seed\"], {\"circles\": {\"P1\": \"family\"}})\r\n            self.assertEqual(content[\"messages\"][0][\"id\"], \"M1\")\r\n            self.assertTrue(content[\"windows\"])\r\n            self.assertEqual(json.loads(result[\"model_output\"]), self.assertEqual(before[field], after[field], field)\r\n\r\n    def test_allowed_input_change_affects_requests_and_output(self):\r\n        before = self.run_probe()\r\n        path = self.folder / after[\"output\"])\r\n        self.assertEqual(before[\"model_output\"], after[\"model_output\"])\r\n\r\n    def test_guard_rejects_unapproved_opens_even_when_caught(self):\r\n No challenge answer files or model calls are used.\"\"\"\r\n\r\nimport copy\r\nimport json\r\nimport shutil\r\nimport subprocess\r\nimport sys\r\nimport unittest\r\nimport uuid\r\nfrom pathlib import Path\r\nfrom unittest.mock import patch\r\n\r\nPROJECT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]\r\nsys.path.insert(0, str(PROJECT))\r\nfrom data import (MessageSpan, ProcessingWindow, WindowPlan, build_windows,\r\n                  load_input, validate_window_coverage)  # noqa: E402\r\n\r\n\r\ndef chat(cid=\"C1\", participants=None):\r\n    return {\"chat_id\": cid, \"name\": \"Synthetic chat\", \"is_group\": True,\r\n            \"participants\": participants if participants is not None else [\"ME\", \"P1\"]}\r\n\r\n\r\ndef message(mid=\"M1\", cid=\"C1\", sender=\"P1\", ts=100):\r\n    return {\"id\": mid, \"chat_id\": cid, \"sender_id\": sender, \"sender_name\": \"Synthetic person\",\r\n            \"ts\": ts, \"kind\": \"text\", \"body\": \"Original text\", \"quoted_id\": None}\r\n\r\n\r\nclass InputTests(unittest.TestCase):\r\n    def setUp(self):\r\n        # Use inherited permissions; Python's private Windows temp directories can\r\n        # exclude the sandbox identity. self.folder)\r\n\r\n    def write_inputs(self, chats=None, messages=None, seed=None):\r\n        chats = [chat()] if chats is None else chats\r\n        messages = [message()] if messages is None else messages\r\n        seed = {} if seed is None else seed\r\n        for filename, records in ((\"chats.jsonl\", chats), (\"messages.jsonl\", messages)):\r\n            (self.folder / filename).write_text(\r\n                \"\".join(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) + \"\\n\" for r in records), encoding=\"utf-8\")\r\n        (self.folder / \"seed.json\").write_text(json.dumps(seed, ensure_ascii=False), encoding=\"utf-8\")\r\n\r\n    def codes(self, result):\r\n        return [issue.code for issue in result.issues]\r\n\r\n    def test_indexes_and_chronology_across_chats(self):\r\n        records = [message(\"late\", ts=300), message(\"other\", \"C2\", \"P2\", 150),\r\n                   message(\"early\", ts=100), message(\"tie\", ts=100)]\r\n        self.write_inputs([chat(), chat(\"C2\", [\"ME\", \"P2\", \"P3\"]), chat(\"C3\")], records)\r\n        result = load_input(self.folder)\r\n self.assertEqual(result.messages_by_sender[\"P3\"], [])\r\n        self.assertEqual(result.messages_by_sender[\"ME\"], [])\r\n        self.assertEqual(result.person_ids, (\"P1\", \"P2\", \"P3\"))\r\n        self.assertIs(result.messages_by_id[\"early\"], result.messages_by_chat[\"C1\"][0])\r\n        self.assertIs(result.messages_by_id[\"other\"], result.messages_by_sender[\"P2\"][0])\r\n        self.assertEqual(result.as_of, 300)\r\n        self.assertEqual(result.summary()[\"messages_indexed\"], 4)\r\n        result.require_complete()\r\n\r\n    def open must not touch it.\\r\\n        return os.path.normcase(os.path.abspath(os.fsdecode(path)))\\r\\n\\r\\n    allowed_reads = {key(folder / name) for name in INPUTS}\\r\\n    output = folder / \\\"output.json\\\"\\r\\n    usage = folder / \\\"usage.jsonl\\\"\\r\\n    allowed_writes = {key(output), key(usage)}\\r\\n    report = {\\\"reads\\\": [], \\\"writes\\\": [], \\\"imports\\\": [], \\\"violations\\\": [],\\r\\n              \\\"requests\\\": [], \\\"ok\\\": False}\\r\\n    active = True\\r\\n    phase = \\\"initialization\\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n    def deny(operation):\\r\\n        report[\\\"violations\\\"].append(operation)\\r\\n        raise AssertionError(\\\"Forbidden boundary operation: \\\" + operation)\\r\\n\\r\\n    def audit(event, args):\\r\\n        if not active:\\r\\n            return\\r\\n        if event == \\\"open\\\":\\r\\n            path, mode, flags = args\\r\\n            if not isinstance(path, (str, bytes, os.PathLike)):\\r\\n                deny(\\\"open:file-descriptor\\\")\\r\\n            target = key(path)\\r\\n            # O_ACCMODE is not exported on Windows; these access bits are.\\r\\n            access = flags & (os.O_WRONLY | os.O_RDWR)\\r\\n            writing = access != os.O_RDONLY or flags & (os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC | os.O_APPEND)\\r\\n            if writing:\\r\\n                if target not in allowed_writes or access != os.O_WRONLY:\\r\\n                    deny(\\\"open:unapproved-write\\\")\\r\\n                report[\\\"writes\\\"].append([phase, Path(path).name])\\r\\n            else:\\r\\n                if target not in allowed_reads:\\r\\n                    deny(\\\"open:unapproved-read\\\")\\r\\n                report[\\\"reads\\\"].append([phase, Path(path).name])\\r\\n        elif event in {\\\"os.listdir\\\", \\\"os.scandir\\\", \\\"glob.glob\\\", \\\"glob.glob/2\\\",\\r\\n                       \\\"pathlib.Path.glob\\\", \\\"pathlib.Path.rglob\\\", \\\"os.walk\\\", \\\"os.fwalk\\\"}:\\r\\n            deny(\\\"scan:\\\" + event)\\r\\n        elif event == \\\"import\\\" and \\\"check\\\" in args[0].split(\\\".\\\"):\\r\\n            deny(\\\"import:check\\\")\\r\\n        elif event in {\\\"socket.connect\\\", \\\"socket.getaddrinfo\\\", \\\"subprocess.Popen\\\", \\\"os.system\\\"}:\\r\\n            deny(\\\"external:\\\" + builtins.__import__\\r\\n    original_import_module = importlib.import_module\\r\\n\\r\\n    def check_import(name):\\r\\n        if active:\\r\\n            report[\\\"imports\\\"].append(name)\\r\\n     return original_import(name, *args, **kwargs)\\r\\n\\r\\n    def guarded_import_module(name, *args, **kwargs):\\r\\n        check_import(name)\\r\\n        return original_import_module(name, *args, **kwargs)\\r\\n\\r\\n    def fixed_transport(request, **kwargs):\\r\\n        report[\\\"requests\\\"].append(request.data.decode(\\\"utf-8\\\"))\\r\\n        return io.BytesIO(json.dumps({\\\"choices\\\": [{\\\"message\\\": {\\r\\n            \\\"content\\\": json.dumps(FIXED_REPLY)}}],\\r\\n            \\\"usage\\\": {\\\"prompt_tokens\\\": 12, \\\"completion_tokens\\\": 8}}).encode(\\\"utf-8\\\"))\\r\\n\\r\\n    sys.addaudithook(audit)\\r\\n    try:\\r\\n        with patch.object(builtins, \\\"__import__\\\", guarded_import), \\\\\\r\\n {\\r\\n                \\\"builtin_open\\\": lambda: open(decoy, \\\"rb\\\"),\\r\\n                \\\"io_open\\\": lambda: io.open(decoy, \\\"rb\\\"),\\r\\n                \\\"path_open\\\": lambda: \\\"exec_check\\\": lambda: exec(compile(\\\"pass\\\", str(folder / \\\"check.py\\\"), \\\"exec\\\")),\\r\\n            }\\r\\n            if attack == \\\"cached_check\\\":\\r\\n The original architecture report, Session 01 records, and existing transcript contents remain unchanged.\r\n\r\n**Recommendation: six runtime modules, one model, and a task ledger.**\r\n\r\nThe previous architecture has useful ideas but splits them into too many files for a first implementation. Dev goals/help are unlabelled. No additional accuracy target, spending limit, provider feature, or benchmark result is claimed here.\r\n\r\nThis plan deliberately replaces the earlier illustrative price and latency examples with formulas driven by measured request usage and elapsed time. Model, account limits, prices, and tokenizer behavior remain unknown until verified for the configured endpoint.\r\n\r\n**1. Remaining modules and their boundaries.**\r\n\r\nAll paths below are proposed future implementation paths relative to the starter project. Does not silently mutate task state. No separate module per output category. |\r\n| `tasks.py` â€” new | Store task records and chronological events; generate candidate matches; request semantic review through `reasoning.py`; validate and apply supported operations; produce final task states. | Owns task identity/state. Similar titles or shared source IDs alone do not merge tasks. |\r\n| `llm.py` â€” existing | Be the single HTTP client; read required environment variables; retain response metadata; track calls/tokens/time; perform bounded retries; deduplicate identical successful requests in memory. | Only runtime network boundary. No second endpoint/model and no duplicate retry layer. |\r\n| `validation.py` â€” new | Check intermediate response structure, evidence references, final schema rules, and semantic invariants. | Never imports `check.py` or any evaluator. Does not repair meaning by inventing values. |\r\n\r\nKeep `run.sh`'s public interface, `schema.json`, and `check.py`. Do not change the schema or checker to accommodate predictions. Tests can use the standard-library test runner and synthetic fixtures. A separate evaluation framework is unnecessary for version 1: after approval, the supplied checker can evaluate frozen outputs as an explicit separate command.\r\n\r\nDependency direction: `solution.py` coordinates the other modules; `tasks.py` may call the task-review function in `reasoning.py`; `reasoning.py` uses `llm.py` and `validation.py`; `data.py` and `validation.py` do not import semantic stages. This avoids circular dependencies. Use ordinary dictionaries and a few small record definitions near their owning module rather than a separate type hierarchy.\r\n\r\nPrefer an already available, compatible offline schema dependency over writing a validator unnecessarily. `validation.py` should be a thin adapter around that dependency plus the project's evidence, coverage, date, and task-state checks. Read the actual `schema.json`; do not copy its enums into a second contract. This does not add a seventh application module.\r\n\r\nRead-only inspection found local Windows package metadata for `jsonschema` 4.26.0, requiring Python >=3.10 and transitive dependencies including `attrs`, `jsonschema-specifications`, `referencing`, and `rpds-py`. This confirms local metadata presence only: no import was executed, and neither working imports nor availability on the target Linux environment has been verified. The starter allows Python 3.9 or later, so this installed version must not silently raise the submission's minimum Python version or be assumed portable from Windows.\r\n\r\nMake one packaging decision before implementing validation: if a compatible, pinned validator and its dependencies are available offline on the chosen supported Linux interpreter, use it and verify it with network disabled. Do not copy platform-specific Windows dependencies into Linux or install from the internet during grading. Verify use of Draft 2020-12 and keep schema resolution local. No format extras are needed for this schema; project date/time checks remain explicit in Python because the supplied schema does not enforce those formats.\r\n\r\nOnly if that offline dependency cannot be supplied practically, choose a small custom validator for the exact used vocabulary: `type`, `enum`, `required`, `properties`, `additionalProperties`, `items`, `minItems`, `maxItems`, and `oneOf`. Treat title/description/schema-identification fields as metadata and fail clearly on unsupported validation keywords. Test every rule, nullable values, nested objects, and the exactly-one chat target. This is a scoped implementation, not full JSON Schema support. Do not build both validators for production or silently switch when an import fails: record the chosen backend/version, and fail configuration if that backend is unavailable. An already working local dependency can serve as a development reference for synthetic cases if the custom route is chosen, without becoming a runtime dependency.\r\n\r\n**2. Exact end-to-end data flow.**\r\n\r\nThe proposed normal run has the following order. Model stages are bounded batches, not one call per person or message.\r\n\r\n| Step | Input â†’ operation â†’ result | Model calls? |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| 0 | Arguments/environment â†’ validate configuration, start monotonic deadline, create empty budget ledger â†’ run context. | None; an approved compatibility check can be a separately counted setup job. |\r\n| 1 | `chats.jsonl`, `messages.jsonl`, `seed.json` â†’ validate, preserve raw text/IDs, index, sort â†’ input store and end-of-data time. | None. |\r\n| 2 | Per-chat messages â†’ bounded windows with overlap and explicit primary coverage â†’ extraction-job manifest and initial call/token estimate. | None. |\r\n| 3 | Each window plus relevant seed facts â†’ shared extraction â†’ relationship/organisation observations, goal candidates, task creations, and task-update events. | One request per window/job, plus bounded recovery. |\r\n| 4 | Valid observations â†’ merge overlap duplicates and build compact evidence packets â†’ global observation store. | None. |\r\n| 5a | Organisation observations plus seed names â†’ reconcile actual organisations and aliases â†’ canonical organisation records and IDs. | Batched context-resolution requests. |\r\n| 5b | Person/chat observations plus canonical organisations â†’ resolve circles, links, care relationships, and chat targets â†’ people and chat arrays. | Batched context-resolution requests; direct-chat defaults and seed circles use Python. |\r\n| 5c | Goal observations plus original evidence â†’ merge, reject unsupported intentions, choose allowed categories â†’ at most ten goals. | Batched context-resolution requests; skip only if extraction produced no candidates and coverage succeeded. |\r\n| 6 | Task creations/update events plus resolved people/chat context â†’ same-chat matching, cross-chat review, chronological replay â†’ task ledger with final states. | Batched task-review requests only where interpretation is needed. |\r\n| 7 | Final open tasks plus responsibility/context â†’ assist/remind/leave decisions â†’ help details attached to open tasks. | Batched final-decision requests. Closed tasks require none. |\r\n| 8 | All five results â†’ field allowlist, actual-schema validation, semantic checks, stable sorting â†’ one validated JSON object. | None. |\r\n| 9 | Validated JSON â†’ temporary file beside requested output â†’ final validation and atomic replacement â†’ output file and concise run diagnostics. | None. |\r\n\r\nSteps 5aâ€“5c use functions and variants of one context-resolution prompt family, not three separate subsystems. Task-review and help requests share another small prompt family but keep their result types distinct. The shared extraction pass is the only routine pass over all raw messages. Later requests contain compact observations and just the relevant original evidence.\r\n\r\nLarge decision sets are partitioned by entities or chats. Cross-partition organisation aliases receive a bounded merge review before IDs are finalised. People can legitimately link to several organisations; conflicting profile observations are reviewed rather than letting the last response overwrite earlier facts. A final consistency sweep checks duplicate organisations and conflicting chat targets. No unbounded recursive merging loop is allowed.\r\n\r\n**3. Minimal input/evidence contract.**\r\n\r\nThe loader explicitly opens only the three allowed input filenames. It does not enumerate the input directory, search for JSON files, check whether an answers directory exists, or import `check.py`. Seed facts are allowed input; reference labels are not. Resolve the allowed filenames to regular input files, and reject paths redirected into an answers directory. Inference modules receive parsed records, not an unrestricted dataset-directory handle they can search later.\r\n\r\nRetain original message IDs, chat IDs, sender IDs, timestamps, kind, text, and quoted ID. Sort by timestamp with original input position as a stable tie-breaker. A tie-breaker makes processing reproducible; it does not prove a real temporal order between simultaneous events. Set `as_of` to the latest input timestamp. Use a separately documented timezone policy for relative dates; Asia/Kolkata remains an assumption to confirm, not a fact inferred from every message.\r\n\r\nA window contains primary messages to extract and a small amount of overlapping context. Every message or span of a very long message has exactly one primary assignment; overlap does not count as additional coverage. Long text is split into spans retaining the same message ID, not cut off silently. Missing replies remain null. Media placeholders provide no attachment contents. Keep English, Hindi, Bengali, and Hinglish in their original form; no whole-dataset translation stage is needed.\r\n\r\nEach observation contains its category, subject/entity IDs, source message IDs, a short supporting span where useful, and its proposed meaning. Unknown source IDs and citations outside the supplied/retrieved context are rejected. Keep all provenance locally; final output includes only fields permitted by the real schema. Model instructions must treat chat text as data, including any instructions embedded in a conversation.\r\n\r\nThere are only two important long-lived derived stores: a compact context store for people/organisations/chats/goals, and a task ledger. Keep request counters, coverage states, and validation errors in a small run record. No database or persistent cache is needed.\r\n\r\n**4. All five categories in the first version.**\r\n\r\n| Category | Minimum real implementation |\r\n|---|---|\r\n| People | Start from all non-`ME` participants, preserve supplied seed circles, aggregate evidence across chats, choose one allowed circle, and produce supported organisation/care links. |\r\n| Organisations | Discover entities the user runs/belongs to; combine supported aliases/chats/people; infer required type/side/role; do not create an organisation for every group or client company. |\r\n| Chats | Assign every chat exactly once. Use the other participant as a direct-chat default where appropriate; infer group purpose and allow group-to-person assignments. |\r\n| Goals | Distinguish the user's supported habits, milestones, and experiences from ordinary tasks/forwarded content; merge duplicates; choose area/type/horizon and real evidence; output no more than ten. |\r\n| Open loops | Extract obligations/questions, resolve owners, reconcile updates, preserve done/dropped tasks and closure evidence, and choose help for final open tasks. |\r\n\r\nThe schema has no unknown circle or unknown chat target. For uncertainty, request the best-supported allowed decision with an internal low-support flag and, if budget permits, one focused review. Do not confuse a model's forced choice with a verified fact. If no defensible required record can be produced after failures, the run is incomplete; do not invent an organisation or treat a blanket friend assignment as a successfully inferred result. Optional unsupported links/goals are omitted with diagnostics. Any emergency fallback would need to be explicit and would not pass the normal completion gate.\r\n\r\nFor help, retain all three choices. `assist` includes a useful proposed step and whether that step needs permission; `remind` includes a time based on evidence and a documented timing policy; `leave` explains why intervention is unsuitable. Closed loops have no help object. The program writes recommendations, not external actions. Use null for unjustified due dates. A reminder-time policy is not a claimed conversation fact.\r\n\r\n**4a. Deterministic selection of at most ten goals.**\r\n\r\nThe LLM interprets candidate meaning and proposes supporting messages. Python performs eligibility checks, calculates a ranking key from validated evidence, and takes the first ten. Selection is deterministic for the same validated candidate/evidence records; this does not claim that fresh LLM extraction itself is deterministic.\r\n\r\nFirst require an actual goal rather than only an immediate task, evidence that the user owns/adopts the intention or practice, at least one existing supporting message ID, and valid area/type/horizon fields. A seed goal without message evidence is context only. Reject forwarded promotions, unsupported other-person goals, and guesses with reason codes. Candidates awaiting a failed review are pending, not rejected. Unresolved semantic uncertainty that has actually been reviewed can be rejected for insufficient evidence, with its sources retained in diagnostics.\r\n\r\nMerge candidates representing the same intention/outcome before ranking. Do not merge by area alone, and do not treat shared evidence IDs as sufficient proof of equivalence. Retain the union of genuine supporting evidence for each merged goal. Exact duplicate observations from overlap are counted once. Repeated copies, quotations, or forwards of one utterance do not create independent support. A concise original supporting span is used for deduplication; raw text and original IDs remain intact.\r\n\r\nProposed starting recovery policy: reserve up to 20% of planned base calls for extra requests, with one shared cap across retries, repair, and splitting. Each original job may generate at most three outbound transmissions in total, including its split children. This prevents a retry tree from growing exponentially. These are configurable engineering starting values, not provider limits. If a failed original call is split into two children, those three transmissions exhaust that job's allowance; the children do not each receive fresh retry budgets.\r\n\r\n**9. Planning for the 30-minute limit.**\r\n\r\nUse a monotonic clock for elapsed runtime, independent of message timestamps. Proposed schedule: all model I/O must finish by minute 27; target process exit by minute 29:30, leaving 30 seconds before the external limit. These are time allocations, not claims that the work fits. Successful full coverage must be demonstrated later on a cold run.\r\n\r\nEstimate each stage using its actual/forecast job count N, conservative observed request latency L, concurrency c, and any verified requests-per-minute or tokens-per-minute limits. A useful planning expression is:\r\n\r\nStage time estimate = the largest of request-wave time, rate-limit time, and the longest dependent request chain, plus backoff allowance.\r\n\r\nRequest-wave time is approximately ceil(N / c) Ã— L. Request-rate time is 60 Ã— N / allowed requests per minute. Token-rate time is 60 Ã— planned tokens / allowed tokens per minute. Use the provider's actual accounting definition if input/output quotas differ. Dependent task updates cannot be accelerated merely by increasing c. Add stage estimates at dependency barriers and include local processing, recovery, and export reserves. This is a forecast, not a hard proof; small latency samples are especially uncertain.\r\n\r\nIf quota limits are unknown, do not assume unlimited throughput. Use measured successful throughput and retain an explicit unknown-rate-limit warning in the run diagnostics. Start with one in-flight request; permit small bounded concurrency only after endpoint tests and the forecast show it is necessary and safe. Even with concurrency, completed responses are merged in stable job order.\r\n\r\nBefore dispatching every request, check the remaining token reservation, remaining recovery allowance, request deadline, and estimated mandatory downstream work. Reforecast after each batch. If the forecast no longer fits, first remove optional second opinions and duplicate context, then repack only within verified context/output limits. Do not silently discard input messages, final reconciliation, or all goals/help to meet the clock. If essential work still cannot fit, mark the run infeasible/incomplete and report which work remains.\r\n\r\nA socket timeout alone is not a reliable total wall-clock deadline. `llm.py` should run blocking HTTP in a small worker process controlled by the parent. Start with one worker. The parent sets an absolute per-attempt deadline, requests termination, waits only a bounded grace interval, escalates to a forceful kill if necessary, and reaps the child with a bounded wait. It must also close its communication handles and avoid waiting on a blocked queue feeder at shutdown. Threads with a timeout on waiting alone would not provide that guarantee. Section 9a specifies how to test this rather than assuming it works.\r\n\r\nUse per-attempt timeout no greater than the remaining network allowance. Reject new calls that cannot reasonably finish with their downstream reserve intact. At the network cutoff, terminate in-flight work, preserve already validated results, and finish local diagnostics. Publish the designated final output only on overall success as defined in section 4b. Optional partial output uses a separate marked path and a non-success outcome; never replace the designated final file with partial results. Existing final output must not be mistaken for a new successful run after failure.\r\n\r\nThe preflight/run summary should report planned versus actual calls per job type, estimated versus reported tokens, unknown usage, latency samples, retries/splits, covered versus failed primary messages, unresolved events, time reserved for downstream work, validation outcome, and completion state. No API secrets or raw private message text belongs in routine diagnostics. These counters are essential; a dashboard and persistent cache are not.\r\n\r\n**9a. Practical local tests and mandatory Linux deadline tests.**\r\n\r\nKeep ordinary local tests fast: inject a fake clock and fake transport, advance time without sleeping for 30 minutes, and assert dispatch decisions, recovery limits, stage states, and cleanup requests. Use a spawn-based process test with a synthetic worker to exercise real child creation/termination on Windows as well. Local Windows success does not establish Linux signal, process-group, shell, or packaging behavior.\r\n\r\nFor Linux, use a clean Linux container or VM with the submission's selected supported Python version and offline dependency bundle. WSL can be convenient for development, but the acceptance record must identify the actual Linux distribution, interpreter, process start method, and launcher used. No such environment test has run yet. Prepare any test image/dependencies before the offline test; disable external networking and use fake transports. Optional loopback-only slow-server fixtures need no model endpoint, credentials, or challenge data.\r\n\r\nThe application deadline covers normal orchestration and HTTP children. To protect against a hung parent, final validation, or serialization, require a launcher-level hard timeout too. Proposed minimal Linux choice: verify a suitable `timeout` utility on the target, then make a small approved future `run.sh` integration that starts the timeout before Python startup, sends termination at 1,770 seconds, and forcefully stops remaining run processes after a two-second grace. Keep workers in the run's process group and prohibit detached children. The public two-argument interface and six Python modules remain unchanged. Do not assume the utility exists or its process-group behavior works: the target test must prove both. If unavailable, Linux readiness fails until an equivalent supervisor is selected and tested; silently omitting this safeguard is not acceptable.\r\n\r\nUse shortened real-time deadlines for integration tests, for example: six-second network cutoff, ten-second application exit target, twelve-second outer termination, one-second force-kill grace, and a separate test-harness emergency timeout at twenty seconds. These are proposed test settings, not measured performance. The harness observes wall time independently of the clock being tested. An overloaded host that violates timing tolerance yields a failed/inconclusive test to investigate, not permission to widen limits until the test passes.\r\n\r\nRequired Linux scenarios are: worker hangs before receiving any body; worker produces slow partial data; worker stalls while transferring its response; retry delay extends past cutoff; child exits/crashes without a valid result; child ignores normal termination so force-kill is exercised; and the main process hangs so the outer launcher must terminate the entire run. Also exercise a normal successful run through the same launcher and an interruption during temporary-file writing.\r\n\r\nRecord run-owned PIDs/process-group IDs, dispatch/termination/reap timestamps, exit outcomes, stage states, and output hashes. Pass only when: no dispatch occurs after cutoff; the worker is reaped within the configured grace plus a declared small scheduling tolerance; no run-owned child remains alive or unreaped; the outer hang case exits no later than outer deadline plus kill grace plus that tolerance and strictly before the harness emergency timeout; forced termination is non-success; and an existing final output remains byte-for-byte unchanged on all incomplete runs. For a proposed two-second test scheduling tolerance, the example outer case must finish by fifteen seconds. This is an acceptance bound to test, not an assertion about the environment.\r\n\r\nTest the production constants as configuration values as well: network cutoff 1,620 seconds, application/launcher termination target 1,770 seconds, force-kill grace two seconds, and total allowance below 1,800 seconds including a documented startup/cleanup margin. The final measured full run remains the scaling check after paid-run authorization; accelerated failure tests prove shutdown mechanics without spending half an hour per local test. Handle output validation and export inside the overall deadline, not as unbounded work after it.\r\n\r\n**10. Failed, incomplete, or malformed responses.**\r\n\r\nAll responses are proposals until validated. Apply the checks in this order: transport outcome; usable/non-refused content; truncation metadata when available; JSON parsing; expected stage fields/types; required item IDs and batch completeness; valid evidence references; semantic consistency. Metadata availability is provider-specific and must be verified. A normal finish indicator does not prove factual completeness.\r\n\r\n| Failure | First-version behavior |\r\n|---|---|\r\n| Temporary timeout, rate limit, or server error | Retry within the job/global recovery and deadline allowances. Respect a supplied retry delay only if it still fits. Bound all backoff. |\r\n| Invalid credentials or permanent configuration error | Fail promptly. Do not burn the recovery budget repeating the same invalid request. |\r\n| Unsupported optional request parameter | Allow a single documented capability correction if verified; otherwise fail configuration. Never silently change the model or endpoint. |\r\n| Empty reply or explicit refusal | Treat the job as failed. At most one appropriately narrower/rephrased attempt within the same caps; repeated refusal is not success or no findings. |\r\n| Malformed JSON or wrong types | One repair request with the exact errors and necessary original context if budget permits. Do not use unrestricted text evaluation or guess missing JSON fragments. |\r\n| Output truncated | Discard the incomplete structure and split the original job once into smaller primary ranges/candidate groups, preserving overlap and references. Both children share the original transmission cap. |\r\n| Valid JSON missing requested people/chats/decision IDs | Preserve independently valid items in memory, request only missing items within budget, and keep the parent job incomplete until required coverage is satisfied. |\r\n| Invented IDs, unsupported citations, impossible owner | Reject affected records. A valid JSON object does not make its evidence real. |\r\n| Conflicting completion/ownership decisions | Retrieve the original evidence for a bounded review; if unresolved, retain the last supported task state rather than guessing closure. |\r\n| Recovery/deadline exhausted | Record failed coverage/unresolved decisions, finish local validation, and report an incomplete run. Never replace the failure with an apparently successful empty category. |\r\n\r\nRequire an acknowledgement of the primary message range or requested entity/event IDs in intermediate results so omissions can be detected. This verifies structural coverage, not semantic recall: an LLM can acknowledge a message and still miss a task. Manual review is needed to measure that error. If a response claims an internal output limit was reached, split the job even if its JSON parses.\r\n\r\nCache only complete validated results in a run-local dictionary keyed by exact request content, stage/schema version, endpoint/model/settings, and relevant context. A corrected seed fact or changed task state must produce a different key. No disk cache or resume framework is needed in version 1. A timeout may have incurred remote billing even if usage was not returned; log it as unknown, not free.\r\n\r\n**11. Minimum tests and acceptance criteria by implementation stage.**\r\n\r\nThese are future tests after approval; none were written or run during planning. Use synthetic messages and a fake HTTP transport/clock first. Deterministic tests validate the code's mechanics. They do not establish real model accuracy. A later small live sample assesses language interpretation, followed by a cold-run rehearsal. Do not require repeated full development runs to test ordinary formatting logic.\r\n\r\n| Stage | Minimum tests | Acceptance gate |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n1:**Session 02 — Revised implementation plan**\n5:**Recommendation: six runtime modules, one model, and a task ledger.**\n15:**1.
