A concise original supporting span is used for deduplication; raw text and original IDs remain intact.\n97:Assign one evidence tier, using the strongest validated support:\n102:| 2 — Observed sustained pursuit | No tier-3 statement, but original `ME` messages on at least two distinct dates support concrete pursuit of the same broader outcome/practice. The wording must support the goal, not merely two unrelated errands. |\n103:| 1 — Indirect but corroborated | No higher-tier evidence, but at least two distinct non-duplicate conversational episodes attribute the goal to the user, and an original `ME` message in the evidence corroborates ownership. Third-party assertions alone are insufficient. |\n105:Candidates meeting none of these tiers are excluded as insufficiently supported; this is a conservative design policy, not an organiser rule. Important one-off explicit aspirations qualify at tier 3. A seed statement alone does not boost a goal's rank or supply missing evidence.\n107:For eligible, deduplicated candidates sort by this exact sequence:\n110:2. Number of distinct dates containing independent positive `ME` support for this goal, capped at three, descending. The cap limits the advantage of frequently repeated work chatter. Use the same configured timezone policy throughout the run and test date-boundary behavior.\n114:6. A stable internal candidate key: earliest primary source span, canonical area/type/horizon, then Unicode-normalised title, ascending. No random UUID or API completion order enters this key.\n116:Take the first min(10, eligible goal count), preserving that order in output. A tie remaining after all fields means equivalent stored candidates should already have merged; reject duplicate internal keys instead of selecting by arrival order. Do not impose area quotas, invent importance scores, use model confidence as a probability, or add irrelevant evidence to inflate rank. Store the ranking components and rejection reasons internally, not as extra final-output fields. Output the genuine supporting evidence IDs, with both sides of any adoption exchange, rather than the whole conversation.\n120:Minimum selection tests: more than ten eligible candidates select exactly the expected ten; fewer than ten are not padded; a one-off explicit goal outranks weaker repetition; duplicate windows/forwarded copies cannot improve rank; permutation of candidate/API-return order cannot change selection; exact ties use the declared stable fields; invalid/unsupported candidates are excluded with reasons; distinct goals in the same area remain separate; adoption cites both original messages.\n122:**4b. Empty results versus incomplete inference.**\n124:Maintain stage state independently of the output arrays. Use `not_started`, `running`, `succeeded`, `succeeded_empty`, `failed`, or `incomplete`. A caught exception must never be translated directly into `[]`. Store required/completed job IDs, primary coverage, proposed/resolved/rejected/pending candidate counts, failure reasons, and semantic uncertainty flags in the run record. Derive each category's state from all its prerequisite stages, not merely its final model response.\n126:`succeeded_empty` means every prerequisite and required primary window completed, every proposed candidate received a valid disposition, and no supported final items remain. It means no supported findings from completed inference, not mathematical proof that no true items exist. `failed` means an unrecovered operational/configuration/validation failure prevented the required result. `incomplete` means some required coverage or decisions remain pending, including time/token cutoff, unresolved malformed output, or skipped candidate-search work. In short:\r\n\r\n```json\r\n{\r\n  \"people\":        [{\"id\": \"P03\", \"circle\": \"staff\", \"links\": [{\"organisation\": \"home\", \"link\": \"supports\"}], \"looks_after\": [\"P04\"]}],\r\n  \"organisations\": [{\"id\": \"home\", \"name\": \"Home\", \"type\": \"residence\", \"side\": \"home\", \"role\": \"owner\"}],\r\n  \"chats\":         [{\"chat_id\": \"C03\", \"organisation\": \"home\"}, {\"chat_id\": \"C01\", \"person\": \"P01\"}],\r\n  \"goals\":         [{\"title\": \"Run the half marathon in January\", \"area\": \"health\", \"type\": \"milestone\", \"horizon\": \"3mo\", \"evidence\": [\"M026\"]}],\r\n  \"open_loops\":    [{\"title\": \"Decide whether to take Zest\", \"type\": \"question\", \"owner\": \"ME\", \"chat_id\": \"C04\",\r\n                     \"source_msg\": \"M016\", \"due\": \"2026-10-26\", \"status\": \"open\", \"closed_by\": null,\r\n                     \"help\": {\"action\": \"remind\", \"remind_at\": \"2026-10-25T10:00:00+05:30\"}}]\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\n## How the checker scores\r\n\r\nThe same rules we use on the hidden set. They cover 74 of the 100 points.\r\n\r\n- **Circles (12):** share of people with the right circle. People whose circle is in `seed.json`\r\n  don't count.\r\n- **Organisations (8):** we pair your organisations with ours by overlap of the chats and people\r\n  attached to each. Extra or missing organisations lower the score.\r\n- **Chats (8):** share of chats tied to the right person, or to your organisation paired with ours.\r\n- **Goals (8):** a goal matches ours when the area is the same and at least one evidence message\r\n  is shared. The score balances how many of yours are right with how many of ours you found. The\r\n  goal in `seed.json` doesn't count.\r\n- **Open loops found (14):** a loop matches ours when your `source_msg` is one of the messages we\r\n  accept for it. The score balances how many of yours are real with how many of ours you found.\r\n- **Open loops closed right (14):** for each loop you found, the right status counts 1. Calling a\r\n  loop done or dropped when it's still open costs 1. Divided by all of our loops.\r\n- **Where to help (10):** for each open loop we labelled, the right action counts 1. Saying\r\n  `assist` or `remind` where we said `leave` costs 1.\r\n\r\nCost (12), the write-up (7) and runs with one command (7) are scored by us.\r\n\r\n## The answers format\r\n\r\n`answers/answers.json` has the same shape as your output, with three differences in `open_loops`\r\n(and, on the hidden set, a `loops_scored_from` time: loops raised before it are not scored):\r\n`source_msgs` lists every message we accept as the source, `closing_msgs` lists the messages that\r\nshow it closed, and `help` is just the action, with a `why`.\r\n\r\n## Cost\r\n\r\nThe hidden set is about 15,000 messages, so plan for that size: batch your calls.\r\n\r\nUse any model with an OpenAI-compatible API. Read `LLM_BASE_URL`, `LLM_API_KEY` and `LLM_MODEL`\r\nfrom the environment. Do not copy platform-specific Windows dependencies into Linux or install from the internet during grading. Verify use of Draft 2020-12 and keep schema resolution local. No format extras are needed for this schema; project date/time checks remain explicit in Python because the supplied schema does not enforce those formats.\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly if that offline dependency cannot be supplied practically, choose a small custom validator for the exact used vocabulary: `type`, `enum`, `required`, `properties`, `additionalProperties`, `items`, `minItems`, `maxItems`, and `oneOf`. Treat title/description/schema-identification fields as metadata and fail clearly on unsupported validation keywords. Test every rule, nullable values, nested objects, and the exactly-one chat target. This is a scoped implementation, not full JSON Schema support. Do not build both validators for production or silently switch when an import fails: record the chosen backend/version, and fail configuration if that backend is unavailable. An already working local dependency can serve as a development reference for synthetic cases if the custom route is chosen, without becoming a runtime dependency.\\r\\n\\r\\n**2. Exact end-to-end data flow.**\\r\\n\\r\\nThe proposed normal run has the following order. Model stages are bounded batches, not one call per person or message.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Step | Input â†’ operation â†’ result | Model calls? |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| 0 | Arguments/environment â†’ validate configuration, start monotonic deadline, create empty budget ledger â†’ run context. | None; an approved compatibility check can be a separately counted setup job. |\\r\\n| 1 | `chats.jsonl`, `messages.jsonl`, `seed.json` â†’ validate, preserve raw text/IDs, index, sort â†’ input store and end-of-data time. | None. |\\r\\n| 2 | Per-chat messages â†’ bounded windows with overlap and explicit primary coverage â†’ extraction-job manifest and initial call/token estimate. | None. |\\r\\n| 3 | Each window plus relevant seed facts â†’ shared extraction â†’ relationship/organisation observations, goal candidates, task creations, and task-update events. | One request per window/job, plus bounded recovery. |\\r\\n| 4 | Valid observations â†’ merge overlap duplicates and build compact evidence packets â†’ global observation store. | None. |\\r\\n| 5a | Organisation observations plus seed names â†’ reconcile actual organisations and aliases â†’ canonical organisation records and IDs. | Batched context-resolution requests. |\\r\\n| 5b | Person/chat observations plus canonical organisations â†’ resolve circles, links, care relationships, and chat targets â†’ people and chat arrays. | Batched context-resolution requests; direct-chat defaults and seed circles use Python. |\\r\\n| 5c | Goal observations plus original evidence â†’ merge, reject unsupported intentions, choose allowed categories â†’ at most ten goals. | Batched context-resolution requests; skip only if extraction produced no candidates and coverage succeeded. |\\r\\n| 6 | Task creations/update events plus resolved people/chat context â†’ same-chat matching, cross-chat review, chronological replay â†’ task ledger with final states. | Batched task-review requests only where interpretation is needed. |\\r\\n| 7 | Final open tasks plus responsibility/context â†’ assist/remind/leave decisions â†’ help details attached to open tasks. | Batched final-decision requests. Closed tasks require none. |\\r\\n| 8 | All five results â†’ field allowlist, actual-schema validation, semantic checks, stable sorting â†’ one validated JSON object. | None. |\\r\\n| 9 | Validated JSON â†’ temporary file beside requested output â†’ final validation and atomic replacement â†’ output file and concise run diagnostics. | None. |\\r\\n\\r\\nSteps 5aâ€“5c use functions and variants of one context-resolution prompt family, not three separate subsystems. Task-review and help requests share another small prompt family but keep their result types distinct. The shared extraction pass is the only routine pass over all raw messages. Later requests contain compact observations and just the relevant original evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\nLarge decision sets are partitioned by entities or chats. Cross-partition organisation aliases receive a bounded merge review before IDs are finalised. People can legitimately link to several organisations; conflicting profile observations are reviewed rather than letting the last response overwrite earlier facts. A final consistency sweep checks duplicate organisations and conflicting chat targets. No unbounded recursive merging loop is allowed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**3. Minimal input/evidence contract.**\\r\\n\\r\\nThe loader explicitly opens only the three allowed input filenames. It does not enumerate the input directory, search for JSON files, check whether an answers directory exists, or import `check.py`. Seed facts are allowed input; reference labels are not. Resolve the allowed filenames to regular input files, and reject paths redirected into an answers directory. Inference modules receive parsed records, not an unrestricted dataset-directory handle they can search later.\\r\\n\\r\\nRetain original message IDs, chat IDs, sender IDs, timestamps, kind, text, and quoted ID. Sort by timestamp with original input position as a stable tie-breaker. A tie-breaker makes processing reproducible; it does not prove a real temporal order between simultaneous events. Set `as_of` to the latest input timestamp. Use a separately documented timezone policy for relative dates; Asia/Kolkata remains an assumption to confirm, not a fact inferred from every message.\\r\\n\\r\\nA window contains primary messages to extract and a small amount of overlapping context. Every message or span of a very long message has exactly one primary assignment; overlap does not count as additional coverage. Long text is split into spans retaining the same message ID, not cut off silently. Missing replies remain null. Media placeholders provide no attachment contents. Keep English, Hindi, Bengali, and Hinglish in their original form; no whole-dataset translation stage is needed.\\r\\n\\r\\nEach observation contains its category, subject/entity IDs, source message IDs, a short supporting span where useful, and its proposed meaning. Unknown source IDs and citations outside the supplied/retrieved context are rejected. Keep all provenance locally; final output includes only fields permitted by the real schema. Model instructions must treat chat text as data, including any instructions embedded in a conversation.\\r\\n\\r\\nThere are only two important long-lived derived stores: a compact context store for people/organisations/chats/goals, and a task ledger. Keep request counters, coverage states, and validation errors in a small run record. No database or persistent cache is needed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**4. All five categories in the first version.**\\r\\n\\r\\n| Category | Minimum real implementation |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| People | Start from all non-`ME` participants, preserve supplied seed circles, aggregate evidence across chats, choose one allowed circle, and produce supported organisation/care links. |\\r\\n| Organisations | Discover entities the user runs/belongs to; combine supported aliases/chats/people; infer required type/side/role; do not create an organisation for every group or client company. |\\r\\n| Chats | Assign every chat exactly once. Use the other participant as a direct-chat default where appropriate; infer group purpose and allow group-to-person assignments. |\\r\\n| Goals | Distinguish the user's supported habits, milestones, and experiences from ordinary tasks/forwarded content; merge duplicates; choose area/type/horizon and real evidence; output no more than ten. |\\r\\n| Open loops | Extract obligations/questions, resolve owners, reconcile updates, preserve done/dropped tasks and closure evidence, and choose help for final open tasks. |\\r\\n\\r\\nThe schema has no unknown circle or unknown chat target. For uncertainty, request the best-supported allowed decision with an internal low-support flag and, if budget permits, one focused review. Do not confuse a model's forced choice with a verified fact. If no defensible required record can be produced after failures, the run is incomplete; do not invent an organisation or treat a blanket friend assignment as a successfully inferred result. Optional unsupported links/goals are omitted with diagnostics. Any emergency fallback would need to be explicit and would not pass the normal completion gate.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor help, retain all three choices. `assist` includes a useful proposed step and whether that step needs permission; `remind` includes a time based on evidence and a documented timing policy; `leave` explains why intervention is unsuitable. Closed loops have no help object. The program writes recommendations, not external actions. Use null for unjustified due dates. A reminder-time policy is not a claimed conversation fact.\\r\\n\\r\\n**4a. Deterministic selection of at most ten goals.**\\r\\n\\r\\nThe LLM interprets candidate meaning and proposes supporting messages. Python performs eligibility checks, calculates a ranking key from validated evidence, and takes the first ten. Selection is deterministic for the same validated candidate/evidence records; this does not claim that fresh LLM extraction itself is deterministic.\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst require an actual goal rather than only an immediate task, evidence that the user owns/adopts the intention or practice, at least one existing supporting message ID, and valid area/type/horizon fields. A seed goal without message evidence is context only. Reject forwarded promotions, unsupported other-person goals, and guesses with reason codes. Candidates awaiting a failed review are pending, not rejected. Unresolved semantic uncertainty that has actually been reviewed can be rejected for insufficient evidence, with its sources retained in diagnostics.\\r\\n\\r\\nMerge candidates representing the same intention/outcome before ranking. Do not merge by area alone, and do not treat shared evidence IDs as sufficient proof of equivalence. Retain the union of genuine supporting evidence for each merged goal. Exact duplicate observations from overlap are counted once. Repeated copies, quotations, or forwards of one utterance do not create independent support. A concise original supporting span is used for deduplication; raw text and original IDs remain intact.\\r\\n\\r\\nProposed starting recovery policy: reserve up to 20% of planned base calls for extra requests, with one shared cap across retries, repair, and splitting. Each original job may generate at most three outbound transmissions in total, including its split children. This prevents a retry tree from growing exponentially. These are configurable engineering starting values, not provider limits. If a failed original call is split into two children, those three transmissions exhaust that job's allowance; the children do not each receive fresh retry budgets.\\r\\n\\r\\n**9. Planning for the 30-minute limit.**\\r\\n\\r\\nUse a monotonic clock for elapsed runtime, independent of message timestamps. Proposed schedule: all model I/O must finish by minute 27; target process exit by minute 29:30, leaving 30 seconds before the external limit. These are time allocations, not claims that the work fits. Successful full coverage must be demonstrated later on a cold run.\\r\\n\\r\\nEstimate each stage using its actual/forecast job count N, conservative observed request latency L, concurrency c, and any verified requests-per-minute or tokens-per-minute limits. A useful planning expression is:\\r\\n\\r\\nStage time estimate = the largest of request-wave time, rate-limit time, and the longest dependent request chain, plus backoff allowance.\\r\\n\\r\\nRequest-wave time is approximately ceil(N / c) Ã— L. Request-rate time is 60 Ã— N / allowed requests per minute. Token-rate time is 60 Ã— planned tokens / allowed tokens per minute. Use the provider's actual accounting definition if input/output quotas differ. Dependent task updates cannot be accelerated merely by increasing c. Add stage estimates at dependency barriers and include local processing, recovery, and export reserves. This is a forecast, not a hard proof; small latency samples are especially uncertain.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf quota limits are unknown, do not assume unlimited throughput. Use measured successful throughput and retain an explicit unknown-rate-limit warning in the run diagnostics. Start with one in-flight request; permit small bounded concurrency only after endpoint tests and the forecast show it is necessary and safe. Even with concurrency, completed responses are merged in stable job order.\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore dispatching every request, check the remaining token reservation, remaining recovery allowance, request deadline, and estimated mandatory downstream work. Reforecast after each batch. If the forecast no longer fits, first remove optional second opinions and duplicate context, then repack only within verified context/output limits. Do not silently discard input messages, final reconciliation, or all goals/help to meet the clock. If essential work still cannot fit, mark the run infeasible/incomplete and report which work remains.\\r\\n\\r\\nA socket timeout alone is not a reliable total wall-clock deadline. `ll…2762 tokens truncated…tion candidate IDs and retain the full candidate list locally. When no supported match is found in the first packet, expand to the remaining relevant candidates subject to budget. Do not report a truncated candidate search as exhaustive. Lexical matching finds candidates; it cannot by itself establish completion or handle every multilingual paraphrase. Cross-chat recall without explicit linking cues remains a known version-1 limitation, not a reason to merge loosely related work.\\n169:**7. Completion, cancellation, and reopening rules.**\\n173:| No task + an established obligation | Open task | Record original source and owner evidence. |\\n174:| Open + acknowledgement, progress, or revised due date | Remains open | Preserve the update; do not assign closure. |\\n175:| Open + fulfilled obligation | Done | `closed_by` points to the message establishing fulfilment of this task's scope. |\\n176:| Open + explicit cancellation or abandonment | Dropped | `closed_by` points to the cancelling message. |\\n177:| Done/dropped + explicit renewal of the same obligation | Open | Add reopening event and clear the current `closed_by`; retain the old closure in internal history. |\\n179:| Any state + ambiguous/unmatched update | Preserve the last supported state | Keep an unresolved event; do not guess closure. |\\n181:Silence, a passed deadline, a checkmark without clear context, or an okay is not automatic completion. Cancellation under negation must not drop a task. Completion applies to the obligation's actual scope: sending proof and receiving payment are different milestones. One completed subtask must not close a larger compound obligation. Reassignment updates the owner only when supported; it does not itself close the task.\\n183:Cross-chat closure is allowed when linked by evidence. `source_msg` must belong to the loop's declared origin chat, but `closed_by` may belong to another chat. Evidence predating the origin cannot normally close that later obligation; handle contradictory timestamps as uncertainty rather than forcing an order. After a late cross-chat match, replay affected history so a late-discovered earlier completion does not overwrite a subsequent reopening.\\n187:**8. API-call and token-budget mechanism.**\\n205:Base call count = E + O + P + G + M + X + H + Q. Total calls = base calls + R. There is no term for a mandatory call per message, person, or task. Jobs are packed until their full request and reserved response fit the configured limits. A stage with no supported candidates needs no model call, but failed extraction must not masquerade as no candidates.\\n207:Before the first request, E is known exactly from the window manifest. Downstream counts are provisional because task density and model output are unknown. Use a short initial tranche of real extraction jobs after execution is approved, count their output observations and tokens, and project a range for the remaining jobs. Those initial results are reused, not discarded benchmark calls. Update projections as each stage's actual candidate list becomes available. Report counts as measured, planned, or projected; do not pretend all downstream work was known before extraction.\\n219:**9. Planning for the 30-minute limit.**\\n227:Request-wave time is approximately ceil(N / c) × L. Request-rate time is 60 × N / allowed requests per minute. Token-rate time is 60 × planned tokens / allowed tokens per minute. Use the provider's actual accounting definition if input/output quotas differ. Dependent task updates cannot be accelerated merely by increasing c. Add stage estimates at dependency barriers and include local processing, recovery, and export reserves. This is a forecast, not a hard proof; small latency samples are especially uncertain.\\n235:Use per-attempt timeout no greater than the remaining network allowance. Reject new calls that cannot reasonably finish with their downstream reserve intact. At the network cutoff, terminate in-flight work, preserve already validated results, and finish local diagnostics. Publish the designated final output only on overall success as defined in section 4b. Optional partial output uses a separate marked path and a non-success outcome; never replace the designated final file with partial results. Existing final output must not be mistaken for a new successful run after failure.\\n237:The preflight/run summary should report planned versus actual calls per job type, estimated versus reported tokens, unknown usage, latency samples, retries/splits, covered versus failed primary messages, unresolved events, time reserved for downstream work, validation outcome, and completion state. No API secrets or raw private message text belongs in routine diagnostics. These counters are essential; a dashboard and persistent cache are not.\\n239:**9a. Practical local tests and mandatory Linux deadline tests.**\\n245:The application deadline covers normal orchestration and HTTP children. To protect against a hung parent, final validation, or serialization, require a launcher-level hard timeout too. Proposed minimal Linux choice: verify a suitable `timeout` utility on the target, then make a small approved future `run.sh` integration that starts the timeout before Python startup, sends termination at 1,770 seconds, and forcefully stops remaining run processes after a two-second grace. Keep workers in the run's process group and prohibit detached children. The public two-argument interface and six Python modules remain unchanged. Do not assume the utility exists or its process-group behavior works: the target test must prove both. If unavailable, Linux readiness fails until an equivalent supervisor is selected and tested; silently omitting this safeguard is not acceptable.\\n253:Test the production constants as configuration values as well: network cutoff 1,620 seconds, application/launcher termination target 1,770 seconds, force-kill grace two seconds, and total allowance below 1,800 seconds including a documented startup/cleanup margin. The final measured full run remains the scaling check after paid-run authorization; accelerated failure tests prove shutdown mechanics without spending half an hour per local test. Handle output validation and export inside the overall deadline, not as unbounded work after it.\\n255:**10. Failed, incomplete, or malformed responses.**\\n257:All responses are proposals until validated. Apply the checks in this order: transport outcome; usable/non-refused content; truncation metadata when available; JSON parsing; expected stage fields/types; required item IDs and batch completeness; valid evidence references; semantic consistency. Metadata availability is provider-specific and must be verified. A normal finish indicator does not prove factual completeness.\\n266:| Output truncated | Discard the incomplete structure and split the original job once into smaller primary ranges/candidate groups, preserving overlap and references. Both children share the original transmission cap. |\\n268:| Invented IDs, unsupported citations, impossible owner | Reject affected records. A valid JSON object does not make its evidence real. |\\n269:| Conflicting completion/ownership decisions | Retrieve the original evidence for a bounded review; if unresolved, retain the last supported task state rather than guessing closure. |\\n270:| Recovery/deadline exhausted | Record failed coverage/unresolved decisions, finish local validation, and report an incomplete run. Never replace the failure with an apparently successful empty category. |\\n274:Cache only complete validated results in a run-local dictionary keyed by exact request content, stage/schema version, endpoint/model/settings, and relevant context. A timeout may have incurred remote billing even if usage was not returned; log it as unknown, not free.\\n276:**11. Use synthetic messages and a fake HTTP transport/clock first. Deterministic tests validate the code's mechanics. They do not establish real model accuracy. No current date is substituted for the dataset endpoint.\\n# Cold Start: starter kit\\r\\n\\r\\nThe challenge is on https://goals.xpert.chat/w/intern. Read it first. This kit gets you running\\r\\nbefore the real data arrives.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What's here\\r\\n\\r\\n| File | What it is |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| `run.sh` | The one command we run: `./run.sh <input_dir> <output.json>`. It must finish in 30 minutes |\\r\\n| `solution.py` | A naive starting point that writes a valid output. It scores badly on purpose. Replace it |\\r\\n| `llm.py` | A tiny client for any OpenAI-compatible API. It logs token use to `usage.jsonl` |\\r\\n| `schema.json` | The exact shape of `output.json` |\\r\\n| `check.py` | Checks your output's shape, and scores it if the folder has answers |\\r\\n| `example/` | A tiny made-up person, Meera: 6 chats, 29 messages, with the answers |\\r\\n| `WRITEUP.md` | The template for your write-up |\\r\\n| `ai-logs/` | Where your AI session transcripts go |\\r\\n\\r\\nPython 3.9 or later. Nothing to install. You can write your solution in any language, as long as\\r\\n`run.sh` runs it on a clean Linux machine.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Try it now\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n./run.sh example out.json\\r\\npython3 check.py example out.json\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe checker prints the score for each part. The naive solution gets the chats right and little\\r\\nelse. That's your starting line.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When the real data arrives\\r\\n\\r\\nDownload it from the challenge page and unzip it inside this folder. It makes `data/dev/` (already in `.gitignore`):\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n./run.sh data/dev out.json\\r\\npython3 check.py data/dev out.json\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n`data/dev` is the first two weeks with their answers. We run your code on the six weeks after, which\\r\\nyou never see, with every name changed again. Open loops are scored on its last two weeks.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The input\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nchats.jsonl      chat_id, name, is_group, participants (person ids; Arjun is \\\"ME\\\")\\r\\nmessages.jsonl   id, chat_id, sender_id, sender_name, ts (unix seconds), kind, body, quoted_id\\r\\nseed.json        what the person told us at signup: some circles, some organisation names, a goal\\r\\nanswers/         dev set only: answers.json\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n`kind` is `text`, `voice_transcript`, `image_caption` or `document`. `quoted_id` is the message\\r\\nbeing replied to, or null.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The output\\r\\n\\r\\nSee `schema.json`, and the challenge page for what each part means. In short:\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\\"people\\\":        [{\\\"id\\\": \\\"P03\\\", \\\"circle\\\": \\\"staff\\\", \\\"links\\\": [{\\\"organisation\\\": \\\"home\\\", \\\"link\\\": \\\"supports\\\"}], \\\"looks_after\\\": [\\\"P04\\\"]}],\\r\\n  \\\"organisations\\\": [{\\\"id\\\": \\\"home\\\", \\\"name\\\": \\\"Home\\\", \\\"type\\\": \\\"residence\\\", \\\"side\\\": \\\"home\\\", \\\"role\\\": \\\"owner\\\"}],\\r\\n  \\\"chats\\\":         [{\\\"chat_id\\\": \\\"C03\\\", \\\"organisation\\\": \\\"home\\\"}, {\\\"chat_id\\\": \\\"C01\\\", \\\"person\\\": \\\"P01\\\"}],\\r\\n  \\\"goals\\\":         [{\\\"title\\\": \\\"Run the half marathon in January\\\", \\\"area\\\": \\\"health\\\", \\\"type\\\": \\\"milestone\\\", \\\"horizon\\\": \\\"3mo\\\", \\\"evidence\\\": [\\\"M026\\\"]}],\\r\\n  \\\"open_loops\\\":    [{\\\"title\\\": \\\"Decide whether to take Zest\\\", \\\"type\\\": \\\"question\\\", \\\"owner\\\": \\\"ME\\\", \\\"chat_id\\\": \\\"C04\\\",\\r\\n                     \\\"source_msg\\\": \\\"M016\\\", \\\"due\\\": \\\"2026-10-26\\\", \\\"status\\\": \\\"open\\\", \\\"closed_by\\\": null,\\r\\n                     \\\"help\\\": {\\\"action\\\": \\\"remind\\\", \\\"remind_at\\\": \\\"2026-10-25T10:00:00+05:30\\\"}}]\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## How the checker scores\\r\\n\\r\\nThe same rules we use on the hidden set. They cover 74 of the 100 points.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Circles (12):** share of people with the right circle. People whose circle is in `seed.json`\\r\\n  don't count.\\r\\n- **Organisations (8):** we pair your organisations with ours by overlap of the chats and people\\r\\n  attached to each. Extra or missing organisations lower the score.\\r\\n- **Chats (8):** share of chats tied to the right person, or to your organisation paired with ours.\\r\\n- **Goals (8):** a goal matches ours when the area is the same and at least one evidence message\\r\\n  is shared. The score balances how many of yours are right with how many of ours you found. The\\r\\n  goal in `seed.json` doesn't count.\\r\\n- **Open loops found (14):** a loop matches ours when your `source_msg` is one of the messages we\\r\\n  accept for it. The score balances how many of yours are real with how many of ours you found.\\r\\n- **Open loops closed right (14):** for each loop you found, the right status counts 1. Calling a\\r\\n  loop done or dropped when it's still open costs 1. Divided by all of our loops.\\r\\n- **Where to help (10):** for each open loop we labelled, the right action counts 1. Saying\\r\\n  `assist` or `remind` where we said `leave` costs 1.\\r\\n\\r\\nCost (12), the write-up (7) and runs with one command (7) are scored by us.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The answers format\\r\\n\\r\\n`answers/answers.json` has the same shape as your output, with three differences in `open_loops`\\r\\n(and, on the hidden set, a `loops_scored_from` time: loops raised before it are not scored):\\r\\n`source_msgs` lists every message we accept as the source, `closing_msgs` lists the messages that\\r\\nshow it closed, and `help` is just the action, with a `why`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Cost\\r\\n\\r\\nThe hidden set is about 15,000 messages, so plan for that size: batch your calls.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse any model with an OpenAI-compatible API. Read `LLM_BASE_URL`, `LLM_API_KEY` and `LLM_MODEL`\\r\\nfrom the environment. When we score, we set them to our own key and the model you named, and count\\r\\nevery token. `llm.py` does this for you and logs what each call used.\\r\\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\\r\\n# The one command we run:  ./run.sh <input_dir> <output.json>\\r\\n# Change what it calls, not how it is called. It must finish in 30 minutes.\\r\\nset -euo pipefail\\r\\nif [ \\\"$#\\\" -ne 2 ]; then\\r\\n  echo \\\"usage: ./run.sh <input_dir> <output.json>\\\" >&2\\r\\n  exit 2\\r\\nfi\\r\\npython3   "aggregated_output": "**8. API-call and token-budget mechanism.**\r\n\r\nKeep one small budget record in the orchestrator and let every HTTP attempt reserve against it. Track calls completed, calls in flight, estimated and reported input/output tokens, time remaining, recovery attempts, and work still required per stage. No stage may call the API outside this path. Stage budgets are accounting entries, not new modules.\r\n\r\nThere are three prompt families: extract evidence, resolve context, and decide task operations/help. Separate job types within those families make the number of calls visible:\r\n\r\n| Count symbol | Jobs counted |\r\n|---|---|\r\n| E | Extraction windows, packed from actual messages. |\r\n| O | Organisation-resolution batches, including necessary cross-partition merge work. |\r\n| P | People/chat-resolution batches. |\r\n| G | Goal-resolution batches. |\r\n| M | Same-chat task-matching batches. |\r\n| X | Cross-chat or unresolved-event review batches. |\r\n| H | Final help-decision batches for open tasks. |\r\n| Q | Any approved compatibility/setup calls. |\r\n| R | Extra retry, repair, or split requests actually sent. |\r\n\r\nBase call count = E + O + P + G + M + X + H + Q. Total calls = base calls + R. There is no term for a mandatory call per message, person, or task. Jobs are packed until their full request and reserved response fit the configured limits. A stage with no supported candidates needs no model call, but failed extraction must not masquerade as no candidates.\r\n\r\nBefore the first request, E is known exactly from the window manifest. Downstream counts are provisional because task density and model output are unknown. Use a short initial tranche of real extraction jobs after execution is approved, count their output observations and tokens, and project a range for the remaining jobs. Those initial results are reused, not discarded benchmark calls. Update projections as each stage's actual candidate list becomes available. Report counts as measured, planned, or projected; do not pretend all downstream work was known before extraction.\r\n\r\nFor a request j, estimate input tokens from the entire serialized message payload, including instructions, schemas, seed facts, IDs, timestamps, overlap, retrieved context, and any repair transcript. Reserve an output allowance separately. If a verified compatible tokenizer is available locally, use it. Otherwise use a clearly labelled conservative text/UTF-8-size proxy and calibrate it against reported prompt usage from actual calls. Do not assume a universal four-characters-per-token conversion, especially for mixed scripts. A byte-based proxy is an estimate, not a guaranteed bound for an unknown API tokenizer.\r\n\r\nMaintain a safety factor from observed estimate-to-usage discrepancies, increasing it when the estimator undercounts. Keep the verified endpoint context/output limits as configuration; if those limits are unknown, disclose that exact fit cannot be guaranteed and verify them before a full run. Do not invent a context size from an OpenAI-compatible label. Provider-reported usage is authoritative for observed consumption; absent usage remains unknown and retains a conservative reservation.\r\n\r\nPlanned token reserve = sum of estimated input tokens plus reserved output tokens for queued jobs, plus a separately visible recovery reserve. Actual reported tokens, unknown-usage reservations, and outstanding reservations must together stay within the configured run token ceiling. Reserve before dispatch to prevent concurrent calls from overspending the same remaining balance. An output cap is not proof that the answer will be complete; truncation is handled explicitly below.\r\n\r\nThe spending ceiling and token ceiling must be configured from the user's approved budget and verified provider pricing. If prices are not supplied, report tokens and leave dollar cost unknown. If rates are supplied, estimate cost by multiplying each billed token category by its corresponding verified rate; do not double-count reasoning tokens already included in completion tokens. No example dollar price or measured cost is asserted by this plan.\r\n\r\nProposed starting recovery policy: reserve up to 20% of planned base calls for extra requests, with one shared cap across retries, repair, and splitting. Each original job may generate at most three outbound transmissions in total, including its split children. This prevents a retry tree from growing exponentially. These are configurable engineering starting values, not provider limits. If a failed original call is split into two children, those three transmissions exhaust that job's allowance; the children do not each receive fresh retry budgets.\r\n\r\n**9. Planning for the 30-minute limit.**\r\n**11. Minimum tests and acceptance criteria by implementation stage.**\r\n\r\nThese are future tests after approval; none were written or run during planning. Use synthetic messages and a fake HTTP transport/clock first. Deterministic tests validate the code's mechanics. They do not establish real model accuracy. A later small live sample assesses language interpretation, followed by a cold-run rehearsal. Do not require repeated full development runs to test ordinary formatting logic.\r\n\r\n| Stage | Minimum tests | Acceptance gate |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| A. Input and evidence (`data.py`) | Mixed-script UTF-8; shuffled messages/timestamp ties; blank lines; conflicting duplicate IDs; unknown chat/sender references; missing reply; media placeholder; long-message spans; zero-message chat. | Every primary message/span is covered once, raw IDs/text preserved, sorting stable, and invalid structural input reported rather than silently lost. |\r\n| B. Review both predicted items and independent conversation windows to find false positives and false negatives; unmatched predictions can also reflect incomplete reference labels. No reference-derived person mapping, acceptable-source list, or help decision is copied into inference prompts or caches.\r\n\r\n**11a. All category paths and evidence protections work | Stages A–G pass with synthetic fixtures, including nonempty valid results for all five categories, all three help actions, seed preservation, evidence-reference checks, deterministic goal selection from more than ten candidates, and the task-state/ownership/cross-chat cases. A valid output full of unexplained empty arrays is not sufficient. |\r\n| 2. Failure is not reported as empty success | The section 4b cases pass. Incomplete/failed stages produce non-success and cannot replace the designated output. Reviewed semantic ambiguity and unprocessed work have different diagnostics. Old output hashes remain unchanged in failure tests. |\r\n| 3. Answer isolation is demonstrated | Synthetic decoy-answer tests and file-open instrumentation show only the three allowed dataset files are read. Changing or removing the decoy has no effect on inference requests/results. No real development answer files are needed for readiness testing, pilot selection, or inference. |\r\n| 4. Offline Linux validation and cleanup work | The selected validator and dependencies work offline on the recorded supported interpreter. All section 9a process/launcher tests pass, including the ignored-termination and hung-parent cases, with no remaining run-owned children. Local Windows results alone cannot satisfy this gate. |\r\n| 5. Local scale and bookkeeping are exercised without model spend | An offline run using deterministic fake responses and a synthetic dataset representative of the expected message count exercises window coverage, batching, event replay, output size, memory, and export. Include one large chat and long messages, not just many tiny chats. Record actual local elapsed time and memory observations. This checks local overhead and shutdown behavior, not live model latency or quality. |\r\n| 6. Obtain at least one valid response for each request family, exercise every output category across the chosen cases, and record observed usage, latency, repair behavior, and available response metadata. Reuse qualifying recent pilot evidence only if the relevant build/configuration is unchanged. |\r\n| 7. The user approves the specific run | Present the exact dataset path/count, model and endpoint identity without secrets, code/configuration/prompt versions, estimated call/token ranges, verified pricing or other enforceable spend controls, maximum authorized spend, hard call/token/time limits, expected output path, and known pilot weaknesses. Obtain explicit full-run approval or point to an existing approval that clearly covers this exact scope and budget. |\r\n\r\nThe live pilot itself must not start without its own approved cap. Prefer synthetic cases first; any use of development messages must be limited to allowed inputs and the configured endpoint. No cached answer labels, manual-review labels, or benchmark findings are supplied as inference facts. The pilot is intended to verify compatibility and identify obvious failures cheaply, not to become a disguised full-dataset run.\r\n\r\nIf returned token usage is absent, do not report a measured token cost. A paid full run can proceed only if its approved maximum spend is still enforceable using verified provider billing/limits or conservative request/output reservations backed by known pricing. Otherwise this gate fails until the uncertainty is resolved. Missing prices must not be replaced with a hypothetical dollar figure. Unknown account quotas and too few latency samples weaken the runtime forecast; disclose them and use a smaller additional approved pilot when necessary.\r\n\r\nKeep one concise readiness record with pass/fail evidence for gates 1–8 and the user's gate-9 authorization. This can be part of the existing run documentation; it needs no extra application module or dashboard. A fully satisfied synthetic checklist should produce readiness-to-request-approval only until explicit budget authority exists. These tests verify the gate's mechanics; they do not fabricate passed real-world tests.\r\n\r\n**12. Essential version 1 versus optional improvements.**\r\n\r\n| Essential first version | Defer until a measured need appears |\r\n|---|---|\r\n| Six modules and the existing two-argument entry point. | A package hierarchy with a separate module for every small responsibility. |\r\n| All five output categories and help decisions. | Multiple models, providers, or independent agents. |\r\n| Raw evidence IDs, full primary coverage, compact context, chronological task ledger. | Vector databases, embedding models, and database persistence. |\r\n| Same-chat matching and evidence-triggered cross-chat matching. | Broad semantic cross-chat search with embeddings. |\r\n| Bounded retries/repair/splitting, one network boundary, hard request deadlines. | Resume/checkpoint infrastructure and provider-specific batch services. |\r\n| Run-local identical-request reuse and usage/time counters. | Persistent result caches, provider-prefix cache optimisation, and dashboards. |\r\n| One selected offline validator backend plus project semantic checks, following section 1. | Parallel production validator implementations or automatic runtime backend switching. |\r\n| Original-language processing and selected raw-evidence retrieval. | Whole-history translation and additional language-detection services. |\r\n| Synthetic regression tests, Linux deadline tests, a bounded approved pilot, and an explicit paid-full-run gate; measured cold-run readiness after approval. | Large automated model leaderboards or repeated full-run tuning without a specific hypothesis. |\r\n\r\nThe practical implementation order is A/B input isolation → C validation/export → D client/budgets → E extraction/context → F task ledger → G help/integration → H1 readiness and full-run approval → H2 authorized full-dataset rehearsal. That order makes errors observable before complex reasoning is added. A small skeleton run with mocked replies precedes paid testing; a separately approved bounded pilot precedes the full-run authorization decision.\r\n\r\n| Component | Deterministic Python responsibility | LLM responsibility |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| People/circles | Apply seed facts, collect evidence across chats, enforce one circle and valid IDs, preserve corrections. | Interpret roles, kinship, work relationships, and conflicting contextual clues. |\r\n| Organisations | Maintain candidate memberships/aliases, stable IDs, merge bookkeeping, uniqueness. | Decide whether the user belongs to/runs an entity; infer type, side, role, and meaningful equivalence. |\r\n| Chat assignment | Guarantee one target per chat, resolve target IDs, supply direct-chat candidates. | Interpret group purpose and ambiguous direct/group ownership. |\r\n| Goals | Gather/deduplicate candidates, enforce ten-item limit and evidence requirements. | Distinguish user intentions from tasks, quotations, promotions, and other people's goals. |\r\n| Loop detection | Ensure coverage, preserve origins, validate candidate records. | Recognise commitments, followups, deadlines, and pending questions in context. |\r\n| Ownership | Resolve explicit speaker/participant IDs and enforce allowed references. | Resolve pronouns, addressees, delegation, and whether an action is actually owed. |\r\n| Task reconciliation | Sort events, preserve histories, enforce valid transitions, exact-match strong references. | Decide whether two observations concern the same task and whether completion/cancellation is supported. |\r\n| Help | Apply final-state and permission constraints; construct/validate times and required details. | Judge useful assistance, personal sensitivity, responsibility, and likely annoyance. |\r\n\r\n**People, organisations, and chat assignment.**\r\n\r\nPeople classification should use a profile assembled from all their chats, not just their display name. Explicit seed circles take precedence over model guesses. Keep contrasting evidence visible rather than letting a summary overwrite it. Do not invent a universal hierarchy such as family always overrides every other role: the output wants one circle, and the user's actual relationship should guide the decision. A later user correction should update the profile and invalidate dependent decisions without discarding unrelated evidence.\r\n\r\nOrganisation discovery begins with seed names and evidence that the user owns, runs, supports, resides in, or belongs to an entity. Use participants, aliases, activity, and chat purpose to propose merges. Shared participants alone are insufficient: the same person can participate in home, work, and social groups. Do not create client companies merely from mentions, and do not force every group into a new organisation. Empty organisation candidates should be reviewed for unsupported invention rather than retained only because a name was extracted.\r\n\r\nAssign deterministic organisation IDs after merging, for example from a stable ordering of supported membership and canonical source IDs, with collision handling. Names remain presentation labels. Avoid model-generated random IDs or IDs derived solely from names, which change in the hidden set.\r\n\r\nResolve this interdependence in a bounded sequence: collect evidence → propose people/organisation candidates → perform initial grouping → assign chats/links → inspect contradictions once. This is preferable to unlimited iterative reconsideration. Shared evidence does not become independent proof merely because two model stages repeated it.\r\n\r\nA normal direct chat has a natural person target, so use that as a strong structural default subject to contrary contextual evidence. For groups, ask whether the group's purpose belongs to the user organisation or to a particular person. A child's parent group can belong to the child. The target must be a real input person or a discovered organisation, not an invented persona. Staff `looks_after` and `supports` links need specific evidence; being in the same group does not establish care or employment.\r\n\r\n**Goal extraction.**\r\n\r\nExtract intentions expressed or adopted by the user, including a conversational reply confirming a goal. Separate a recurring habit, a measurable milestone, and a planned experience from the immediate steps supporting it. A plan to take a family trip may be a goal while booking accommodation is a loop; both can exist with distinct purposes and evidence. A forwarded advertisement or somebody else's ambition does not automatically become the user's goal.\r\n\r\nMerge paraphrases about the same outcome, but do not merge distinct goals merely because they share an area. Select the allowed area/type/horizon from the actual schema. Horizon categories are coarse and the brief does not define exact boundary rules; document a consistent nearest-supported-category policy and flag inferred horizons internally rather than manufacturing dates. Rank supported candidates by evidence and importance, not by a desire to fill ten slots.\r\n\r\nCite the smallest useful set of actual supporting messages. Do not attach all messages to improve the chance of a scorer overlap. Evidence stuffing would conceal errors and destroy the meaning of evidence. If a seed goal lacks message evidence in this input, retain it as internal context instead of emitting an invalid evidence-free output goal.\r\n\r\n**Task detection, ownership, and temporal state.**\r\n\r\nFirst extract obligations and update events broadly enough to retain plausible work, then require evidence before promoting a candidate into the final ledger. Statements about completed past events, hypothetical plans, advertisements, rhetorical questions, and generic social questions need context before being treated as actionable loops. A candidate's source should be the message that actually raised the obligation or pending decision, not automatically the latest reminder.\r\n\r\nPreserve all plausible source evidence internally but export one canonical origin. Choose it by the conversation semantics, never by consulting acceptable answer IDs. A single message can raise multiple tasks; deduplication must consider action/object, owner, and context, not just `source_msg`. Conversely, several messages can update one task. Do not generate a fresh loop for every followup mention.\r\n\r\nResolve ownership from the obligation: first-person promises normally refer to the speaker; requests require resolving the addressee; waiting-on-someone tasks belong to that person. A task's owner is not automatically its sender or `ME`. For unclear group requests, retain ambiguity until more context appears. Prefer precise actions over compound titles that mix different owners or completion criteria.\r\n\r\nThe allowed loop types overlap semantically. Proposed consistent policy: use the main unresolved issue to choose the type. A pending decision is a question; waiting for somebody else's deliverable is a followup; the user's explicit promise is a commitment; a date-driven requirement without a clearer promise/followup is a deadline. Due dates can accompany any type. This is a proposed annotation convention to test, not a new official precedence rule.\r\n\r\nThe task ledger is an event history, not just a final label. Python orders validated events and maintains state. Explicitly supported completion can move open → done; cancellation can move open → dropped. Acknowledgment or revised due date leaves the task open. Expiration alone never closes it. Later evidence may reopen work; after reopening, obsolete closure evidence must not remain as the current `closed_by`.\r\n\r\nCompletion must match the scope of the obligation. Payment initiated is not necessarily payment received if the obligation was receipt. A request to revise a delivered document can be a reopening or a new related task; resolve it from the wording rather than a fixed keyword. A cancellation phrase in a quoted or negated message is not automatically cancellation.\r\n\r\nUse candidate matching in layers: explicit reply/reference and same task identifiers first; then owner, action/object, temporal continuity, and shared context; finally focused LLM adjudication for ambiguous pairs. Search across chats only when there are strong linking cues. Cross-chat completion is possible, so do not require `closed_by` to be in the source chat; require a supported link and sensible chronology instead.\r\n\r\nIllustrative scenario, not development data: one person promises to send a draft, the recipient says okay, a later message says it was sent, and then the recipient requests a revision. The okay is acknowledgement. The sent message may complete the original send obligation. The revision request needs a separately reasoned update/new task decision. The system must keep those observations rather than compress them into an unsupported single done label.\r\n\r\n**Assist, remind, and leave.**\r\n\r\nMake this decision only after task status and ownership are resolved. Done/dropped loops receive no help object. For open loops, ask what useful next step exists, who can perform it, whether it would intrude, and whether external action would need permission.\r\n\r\nUse `assist` when a specific private/reversible step can move the user's work forward, such as preparing a draft or comparing already available options. `needs_yes` refers to that proposed step itself, not every hypothetical later action. Otherwise this gate fails until the uncertainty is resolved. Missing prices must not be replaced with a hypothetical dollar figure. Unknown account quotas and too few latency samples weaken the runtime forecast; disclose them and use a smaller additional approved pilot when necessary.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep one concise readiness record with pass/fail evidence for gates 1–8 and the user's gate-9 authorization. This can be part of the existing run documentation; it needs no extra application module or dashboard. Do not require a successful full paid run before requesting its first authorization: that would be circular. The authorized full run is H2 and supplies the actual end-to-end cost/runtime/coverage evidence for later submission readiness. It may still fail under new workload or service behavior, in which case preserve diagnostics, stop within limits, and do not automatically purchase a rerun.\\r\\n\\r\\nMinimum acceptance-gate tests use recorded/mock readiness results: a missing goal-ranking test, failed Linux cleanup, unresolved mandatory stage, absent pilot authority, over-budget projection, or missing full-run approval must each prevent a full-run-ready decision. A fully satisfied synthetic checklist should produce readiness-to-request-approval only until explicit budget authority exists. These tests verify the gate's mechanics; they do not fabricate passed real-world tests.\\r\\n\\r\\n**12. Essential version 1 versus optional improvements.**\\r\\n\\r\\n| Essential first version | Defer until a measured need appears |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Six modules and the existing two-argument entry point. | A package hierarchy with a separate module for every small responsibility. |\\r\\n| All five output categories and help decisions. | Multiple models, providers, or independent agents. |\\r\\n| Raw evidence IDs, full primary coverage, compact context, chronological task ledger. | Vector databases, embedding models, and database persistence. |\\r\\n| Same-chat matching and evidence-triggered cross-chat matching. | Broad semantic cross-chat search with embeddings. |\\r\\n| Bounded retries/repair/splitting, one network boundary, hard request deadlines. | Resume/checkpoint infrastructure and provider-specific batch services. |\\r\\n| Run-local identical-request reuse and usage/time counters. | Persistent result caches, provider-prefix cache optimisation, and dashboards. |\\r\\n| One selected offline validator backend plus project semantic checks, following section 1. | Parallel production validator implementations or automatic runtime backend switching. |\\r\\n| Original-language processing and selected raw-evidence retrieval. | Whole-history translation and additional language-detection services. |\\r\\n| Synthetic regression tests, Linux deadline tests, a bounded approved pilot, and an explicit paid-full-run gate; measured cold-run readiness after approval. | Large automated model leaderboards or repeated full-run tuning without a specific hypothesis. |\\r\\n\\r\\nThe practical implementation order is A/B input isolation → C validation/export → D client/budgets → E extraction/context → F task ledger → G help/integration →…371 tokens truncated…her an action is actually owed. |\\r\\n| Task reconciliation | Sort events, preserve histories, enforce valid transitions, exact-match strong references. | Decide whether two observations concern the same task and whether completion/cancellation is supported. |\\r\\n| Help | Apply final-state and permission constraints; construct/validate times and required details. | Judge useful assistance, personal sensitivity, responsibility, and likely annoyance. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**People, organisations, and chat assignment.**\\r\\n\\r\\nPeople classification should use a profile assembled from all their chats, not just their display name. Explicit seed circles take precedence over model guesses. Keep contrasting evidence visible rather than letting a summary overwrite it. Do not invent a universal hierarchy such as family always overrides every other role: the output wants one circle, and the user's actual relationship should guide the decision. A later user correction should update the profile and invalidate dependent decisions without discarding unrelated evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\nOrganisation discovery begins with seed names and evidence that the user owns, runs, supports, resides in, or belongs to an entity. Use participants, aliases, activity, and chat purpose to propose merges. Shared participants alone are insufficient: the same person can participate in home, work, and social groups. Do not create client companies merely from mentions, and do not force every group into a new organisation. Empty organisation candidates should be reviewed for unsupported invention rather than retained only because a name was extracted.\\r\\n\\r\\nAssign deterministic organisation IDs after merging, for example from a stable ordering of supported membership and canonical source IDs, with collision handling. Names remain presentation labels. Avoid model-generated random IDs or IDs derived solely from names, which change in the hidden set.\\r\\n\\r\\nResolve this interdependence in a bounded sequence: collect evidence → propose people/organisation candidates → perform initial grouping → assign chats/links → inspect contradictions once. This is preferable to unlimited iterative reconsideration. Shared evidence does not become independent proof merely because two model stages repeated it.\\r\\n\\r\\nA normal direct chat has a natural person target, so use that as a strong structural default subject to contrary contextual evidence. For groups, ask whether the group's purpose belongs to the user organisation or to a particular person. A child's parent group can belong to the child. The target must be a real input person or a discovered organisation, not an invented persona. Staff `looks_after` and `supports` links need specific evidence; being in the same group does not establish care or employment.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Goal extraction.**\\r\\n\\r\\nExtract intentions expressed or adopted by the user, including a conversational reply confirming a goal. Separate a recurring habit, a measurable milestone, and a planned experience from the immediate steps supporting it. A plan to take a family trip may be a goal while booking accommodation is a loop; both can exist with distinct purposes and evidence. A forwarded advertisement or somebody else's ambition does not automatically become the user's goal.\\r\\n\\r\\nMerge paraphrases about the same outcome, but do not merge distinct goals merely because they share an area. Select the allowed area/type/horizon from the actual schema. Horizon categories are coarse and the brief does not define exact boundary rules; document a consistent nearest-supported-category policy and flag inferred horizons internally rather than manufacturing dates. Rank supported candidates by evidence and importance, not by a desire to fill ten slots.\\r\\n\\r\\nCite the smallest useful set of actual supporting messages. Do not attach all messages to improve the chance of a scorer overlap. Evidence stuffing would conceal errors and destroy the meaning of evidence. If a seed goal lacks message evidence in this input, retain it as internal context instead of emitting an invalid evidence-free output goal.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Task detection, ownership, and temporal state.**\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst extract obligations and update events broadly enough to retain plausible work, then require evidence before promoting a candidate into the final ledger. Statements about completed past events, hypothetical plans, advertisements, rhetorical questions, and generic social questions need context before being treated as actionable loops. A candidate's source should be the message that actually raised the obligation or pending decision, not automatically the latest reminder.\\r\\n\\r\\nPreserve all plausible source evidence internally but export one canonical origin. Choose it by the conversation semantics, never by consulting acceptable answer IDs. A single message can raise multiple tasks; deduplication must consider action/object, owner, and context, not just `source_msg`. Conversely, several messages can update one task. Do not generate a fresh loop for every followup mention.\\r\\n\\r\\nResolve ownership from the obligation: first-person promises normally refer to the speaker; requests require resolving the addressee; waiting-on-someone tasks belong to that person. A task's owner is not automatically its sender or `ME`. For unclear group requests, retain ambiguity until more context appears. Prefer precise actions over compound titles that mix different owners or completion criteria.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe allowed loop types overlap semantically. Proposed consistent policy: use the main unresolved issue to choose the type. A pending decision is a question; waiting for somebody else's deliverable is a followup; the user's explicit promise is a commitment; a date-driven requirement without a clearer promise/followup is a deadline. Due dates can accompany any type. This is a proposed annotation convention to test, not a new official precedence rule.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe task ledger is an event history, not just a final label. Python orders validated events and maintains state. Explicitly supported completion can move open → done; cancellation can move open → dropped. Acknowledgment or revised due date leaves the task open. Expiration alone never closes it. Later evidence may reopen work; after reopening, obsolete closure evidence must not remain as the current `closed_by`.\\r\\n\\r\\nCompletion must match the scope of the obligation. Payment initiated is not necessarily payment received if the obligation was receipt. A request to revise a delivered document can be a reopening or a new related task; resolve it from the wording rather than a fixed keyword. A cancellation phrase in a quoted or negated message is not automatically cancellation.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse candidate matching in layers: explicit reply/reference and same task identifiers first; then owner, action/object, temporal continuity, and shared context; finally focused LLM adjudication for ambiguous pairs. Search across chats only when there are strong linking cues. Cross-chat completion is possible, so do not require `closed_by` to be in the source chat; require a supported link and sensible chronology instead.\\r\\n\\r\\nIllustrative scenario, not development data: one person promises to send a draft, the recipient says okay, a later message says it was sent, and then the recipient requests a revision. The okay is acknowledgement. The sent message may complete the original send obligation. The revision request needs a separately reasoned update/new task decision. The system must keep those observations rather than compress them into an unsupported single done label.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Assist, remind, and leave.**\\r\\n\\r\\nMake this decision only after task status and ownership are resolved. Done/dropped loops receive no help object. For open loops, ask what useful next step exists, who can perform it, whether it would intrude, and whether external action would need permission.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse `assist` when a specific private/reversible step can move the user's work forward, such as preparing a draft or comparing already available options. `needs_yes` refers to that proposed step itself, not every hypothetical later action. Preparing a draft can be false; sending it externally would require true. Do not promise unavailable browsing or tools as if they were executed. Output is a proposal only.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse `remind` when the user must personally decide or act and a reminder has a justified time. Anchor timing to the end-of-data time and contextual due date. A proposed local business-hours policy can supply a reproducible time when the text does not specify one, but it must be documented as a policy, not an extracted fact. Do not set reminders in the past or invent a deadline merely to justify a reminder.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse `leave` when the task is being handled by somebody else, is too personal, or intervention is likely to annoy rather than help. Ownership alone is not an absolute rule: the user may legitimately need help following up on another person's obligation. Conservatism should reflect the asymmetric penalty for unwanted intervention, not a blanket leave-for-everything shortcut. Internally record the evidence and reason for the action choice; export only the allowed fields.\\r\\n\\r\\n    def test_original_ids_text_metadata_and_line_bytes_preserved(self):\\r\\n        item = A tie remaining after all fields means equivalent stored candidates should already have merged; reject duplicate internal keys instead of selecting by arrival order. Do not impose area quotas, invent importance scores, use model confidence as a probability, or add irrelevant evidence to inflate rank. Store the ranking components and rejection reasons internally, not as extra final-output fields. Output the genuine supporting evidence IDs, with both sides of any adoption exchange, rather than the whole conversation.\r\n\r\nThis policy prioritises evidential strength, repeated user support, and recency; it does not claim to know the user's true ranking of life priorities. Normal goal eligibility is not restricted to unfinished goals: a supported goal appearing in the history is not removed simply because a milestone was achieved. Original goal evidence and later changes remain available for accurate wording.\r\n\r\nMinimum selection tests: more than ten eligible candidates select exactly the expected ten; fewer than ten are not padded; a one-off explicit goal outranks weaker repetition; duplicate windows/forwarded copies cannot improve rank; permutation of candidate/API-return order cannot change selection; exact ties use the declared stable fields; invalid/unsupported candidates are excluded with reasons; distinct goals in the same area remain separate; adoption cites both original messages.\r\n\r\n**4b. Empty results versus incomplete inference.**\r\n\r\nMaintain stage state independently of the output arrays. Use `not_started`, `running`, `succeeded`, `succeeded_empty`, `failed`, or `incomplete`. A caught exception must never be translated directly into `[]`. Store required/completed job IDs, primary coverage, proposed/resolved/rejected/pending candidate counts, failure reasons, and semantic uncertainty flags in the run record. Derive each category's state from all its prerequisite stages, not merely its final model response.\r\n\r\n`succeeded_empty` means every prerequisite and required primary window completed, every proposed candidate received a valid disposition, and no supported final items remain. It means no supported findings from completed inference, not mathematical proof that no true items exist. `failed` means an unrecovered operational/configuration/validation failure prevented the required result. `incomplete` means some required coverage or decisions remain pending, including time/token cutoff, unresolved malformed output, or skipped candidate-search work. Preserve valid partial work without relabelling the stage as success.\r\n\r\n| Category | Conditions allowing a genuinely completed empty result |\r\n|---|---|\r\n| People | The validated input has no non-`ME` people to represent. A nonempty person registry with no classifications is failed/incomplete, not empty. |\r\n| Chats | The validated input has no chats. Otherwise every chat needs exactly one target, including chats with no messages. |\r\n| Organisations | Organisation discovery and seed/name consideration completed, no supported organisation survives, and all chats/person references can be resolved without an organisation. A failed organisation call is not evidence of no organisations. |\r\n| Goals | Extraction coverage succeeded and all candidates were either validly rejected/deduplicated or none were found. A missing goal-classification response leaves pending candidates. |\r\n| Open loops | Task discovery and event review completed with no supported obligations. All tasks being done/dropped does not make this array empty: those tasks remain in it. Unprocessed origin/update events block a completed-empty claim. |\r\n| Help substage | If task reconciliation succeeded and there are no final open loops, record `succeeded_empty` with reason `no_open_loops`; closed loops remain in the loop output. If open loops exist, every one needs a valid help decision. |\r\n\r\nDistinguish an evidence-based unresolved interpretation from an unprocessed job. For example, after every planned relevant candidate was reviewed, a genuinely ambiguous update may receive the explicit disposition `ambiguous_evidence`, leaving the task's last supported state and a quality flag. That is a completed conservative decision, not a transport failure. If matching stopped before reviewing the required candidate set, it is incomplete. Track these separately so successful execution cannot hide processing gaps or pretend all meaning is certain.\r\n\r\nOverall success requires each required category/substage to be `succeeded` or `succeeded_empty`, complete mandatory coverage, no pending required decisions, and successful final validation. Add no status fields to the challenge JSON. Emit the run status and counts separately through diagnostics. On failure/incompletion return a non-success outcome and do not replace the designated final output. Optional partial artifacts, if enabled later, use a distinct explicitly marked path. An old output file is not evidence of a successful new run; report the current run ID and outcome.\r\n\r\nMinimum status tests: a truly empty synthetic input; nonempty people/chats with missing results; completed extraction with no goals; all goal candidates validly rejected; extraction timeout that returns no records; an omitted entity in otherwise valid JSON; all loops closed; open loops missing help; a reviewed ambiguous update versus a budget-stopped candidate search. The expected stage state and overall exit outcome must be asserted, not just the final array length.\r\n\r\n**5. How task extraction and identity work.**\r\n\r\nUse a small internal task record: internal task ID; original source/chat; canonical action and object; current owner; owner evidence; due date/evidence; ordered event list; current status; current closure ID; and supporting source IDs. Model-generated internal names are replaced with deterministic IDs by Python. Internal event types can include create, acknowledge, update, complete, cancel, and reopen; these are not extra output status values.\r\n\r\nExtraction returns both new obligations and update observations. It can say an update has an unresolved target. This matters when an isolated message says a document was sent but the original promise is in another window. Never discard an update merely because its origin is absent locally. Conversely, a bare completion remark must not manufacture an earlier promise without evidence.\r\n\r\nOne message can create multiple obligations. Split genuinely separate actions or owners into distinct candidates. Two observations from overlapping windows are duplicates only when source/span and task meaning agree; the shared message ID alone is not a deduplication key. Keep multiple evidence messages for one task without emitting a separate task for every reminder.\r\n\r\n    def test_missing_and_unknown_quotes_do_not_invent_evidence(self):\r\n        missing = message(\"missing\")\r\n        missing.pop(\"quoted_id\")\r\n        unknown = message(\"unknown\")\r\n        unknown[\"quoted_id\"] = \"not-in-input\"\r\n        self.write_inputs(messages=[missing, unknown])\r\n        result = load_input(self.folder)\r\n        self.assertEqual(result.status, \"succeeded\")\r\n        self.assertNotIn(\"quoted_id\", result.evidence(\"missing\").original)\r\n        self.assertEqual(result.evidence(\"unknown\").original[\"quoted_id\"], \"not-in-input\")\r\n        self.assertIn(\"unresolved_quote\", self.codes(result))\r\n        with self.assertRaises(KeyError):\r\n            result.evidence(\"not-in-input\")\r\n\r\n    def test_media_placeholders_and_supported_kinds_are_preserved(self):\r\n        records = []\r\n        for index, (kind, body) in enumerate(((\"image_caption\", \"[photo]\"), (\"document\", \"[document]\"),\r\n                                               (\"voice_transcript\", \"Synthetic transcript\"))):\r\n            item = message(str(index))\r\n            item.update(kind=kind, body=body)\r\n            records.append(item)\r\n        self.write_inputs(messages=records)\r\n        result = load_input(self.folder)\r\n        self.assertEqual(result.status, \"succeeded\")\r\n        self.assertEqual([r.original for r in result.message_records], records)\r\n\r\n    def test_invalid_metadata_and_unknown_references_are_accounted_for(self):\r\n        no_id = message()\r\n        no_id.pop(\"id\")\r\n",

Incomplete runs\nreturn nonzero and preserve any previous output.\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\nimport uuid\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom data import load_input\nfrom validation import SchemaValidator, read_json, validate_output\n\n\ndef assemble_output(data):\n    \"\"\"Assembly seam for later inference; never pass placeholders as findings.\"\"\"\n    if data.chats_by_id or data.messages_by_id or any(data.seed.values()):\n        raise ValueError(\"Incomplete: inference is not implemented at Stage C; no output published.\")\n    return {\"people\": [], \"organisations\": [], \"chats\": [], \"goals\": [], \"open_loops\": []}\n\n\ndef export_output(output, data, output_path):\n    \"\"\"Validate, write beside destination, close/revalidate, atomically replace.\n\n    Exclusive creation avoids clobbering another writer's temporary file. No\n    final path is opened for writing. Ordinary failures and KeyboardInterrupt\n    clean up owned temporaries; an uncatchable kill can leave an unpublished temp.\n    \"\"\"\n    validator = SchemaValidator()\n    validate_output(output, data, validator)\n    destination = Path(output_path).absolute()\n    temporary = destination.with_name(\".\" + destination.name + \".\" + uuid.uuid4().hex + \".tmp\")\n    owned = False\n    try:\n        with temporary.open(\"x\", encoding=\"utf-8\", newline=\"\\n\") as stream:\n            owned = True\n            json.dump(output, stream, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2, allow_nan=False)\n            stream.write(\"\\n\")\n            stream.flush()\n            os.fsync(stream.fileno())\n        with temporary.open(encoding=\"utf-8\") as stream:\n            serialized = read_json(stream)\n        validate_output(serialized, data, validator)\n        if serialized != output:\n            raise ValueError(\"Serialized output differs from the validated in-memory output\")\n        os.replace(temporary, destination)\n    finally:\n        if owned:\n            temporary.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n\n\ndef main(input_dir, output_path):\n    try:\n        data = load_input(input_dir)\n        data.require_complete()\n        output = assemble_output(data)\n        export_output(output, data, output_path)\n    except (OSError, ValueError, UnicodeError, RecursionError) as error:\n        print(\"Run failed: \" + str(error), file=sys.stderr)\n        return 1\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    if len(sys.argv) != 3:\n        print(__doc__, file=sys.stderr)\n        sys.exit(2)\n    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]))\n'@ | Set-Content -Encoding UTF8 solution.py"

inner.stream = stream\\n+                def __enter__(inner):\\n+                    return inner\\n+                def __exit__(inner, *args):\\n+                    inner.stream.close()\\n+
